# Compiler behavior specification

This document records implemented behavior with executable Zig regression evidence.
Each stable ID has one primary `// spec:` test. Additional tests and Python
acceptance drivers exercise further cases; these links are not a claim of exhaustive
coverage or proof of memory safety. The language remains experimental. Native
code is the only runtime (QBE by default, LLVM by explicit build selection): every behavior below is native behavior, and
bytecode names the internal IR it consumes rather than anything that is executed.
The roadmap in `docs/experiments/roadmap.md` tracks unsupported and planned features.

## Core semantics

- [BYTE-LITERAL-1] Quoted byte literals produce ordinary fixed `[N]u8` arrays without terminators. UTF-8 source contributes its encoded bytes; escapes are `\n`, `\r`, `\t`, `\0`, `\\`, `\"` and exactly two hexadecimal digits after `\x`. Empty literals have length zero; each literal is limited to 65,536 decoded bytes (`E_BYTE_LITERAL_LIMIT`). Checking, generic bodies, module relocation, cached syntax and formatting preserve ordinary array identity and behavior. Invalid escapes and unterminated literals are located source errors.

- [CORE-1] Evaluates multiplication before addition, honors parentheses, and associates subtraction from the left.
- [CORE-2] Reports checked arithmetic overflow at the responsible operator without evaluating arithmetic during checking.
- [CORE-3] Returns located source diagnostics for empty input, malformed syntax, and out-of-range integer literals.
- [CORE-4] Accepts the signed and unsigned integer width limits and rejects literals above each maximum.
- [CORE-5] Constructs records by field name and preserves original values when producing updated record copies.
- [RECORD-DEFAULT-1] A record field may declare an immutable scalar default after `=`; a constructor call may omit that field and the default is materialized, positional and named construction stay in declaration order, and a required field after a defaulted one rejects like a function parameter. Enum-keyed tables keep requiring every key.
- [RECORD-UPDATE-1] `replace(record, field=value, …)` returns a copy of the record with only the named fields changed and every other field preserved; the base evaluates before the values, the result is a fresh record value, and the positional `replace(list, index, value)` intrinsic is unchanged.
- [CORE-6] Preserves fixed-array copies and accepts each integer index width, including zero-length arrays.
- [CORE-7] Rejects interchange between distinct nominal domains and requires their declared construction operations.
- [DOMAIN-EQUALITY-1] Values of the same scalar domain support == and != by their integer or boolean backing values, including generic equality requirements. Declaration identity must match across module aliases; equality exposes no backing value, permits no implicit arithmetic or ordering, evaluates operands left to right, and preserves VM cleanup and execution limits.
- [CORE-8] Rejects nonexhaustive matches after a variant is added, including matches on statically known values.
- [CORE-9] Uses enum case names for table construction and indexing regardless of declaration or initializer order.
- [CORE-10] Rejects silent result abandonment and permits `_ = value` to release the result obligation explicitly. A diagnostic about a binding that cannot be assigned or modified names that rule rather than a declaration keyword.
- [CORE-11] Unwraps successful results and propagates compatible error payloads by case name across nominal result types. Native propagation constructs the outgoing result before releasing unfinished operands; failed construction releases the propagated payload before those operands.
- [TRY-CONVERT-1] `?` propagating a result whose error type is the payload of a case of the enclosing function's error union converts implicitly: the error value is wrapped in that case, the outgoing tag is the case's tag, and a payload type that is not a case of the enclosing union still rejects with a located type error.
## Ownership and capabilities

- [OWN-9] Releases previously acquired resources exactly once when a later acquisition propagates an error.
- [OWN-10] Delegates environment access through explicit grants and distinguishes missing, empty, and present values.
- [OWN-11] Rejects direct and transitive allocation that violates declared effect bounds and reports a call witness.
## Projects and native output

- [QBE-PROLOGUE-1] Generated code tests a trap by loading `ctx->trap` through a prologue address instead of calling `myl_qbe_failed`, and a function emits the heap pointer, the root mark and the root restore only when one of its instructions has an opcode that can allocate. A rooted function whose opcodes cannot allocate at all — no call, no allocation — pushes no roots and carries none of that prologue, while its locals stay reachable because no collection can run while it executes. An unused heap pointer is dropped after emission. Which rooted instructions actually push is refined by `QBE-SAFEPOINT-1`.
- [QBE-SAFEPOINT-1] A rooted instruction pushes the live roots only when its own lowered body can call something that allocates: an opcode that admits allocation in one variant but not the one being lowered — a byte-string element read, a scalar widening cast, a checked `+` — runs with no root push and no root restore around it, because its text names no allocating callee. Every guest call, collection constructor, print sink and provider read still pushes, so no value reachable at a collection point becomes unreachable, and trap behavior, results and collection timing are unchanged.
- [QBE-PURECALL-1] A direct guest call pushes roots only when the callee can allocate, directly or transitively: a function whose emitted body reaches no allocating call — an integer predicate, a cursor advance, a field access — is called with the root chain left as the caller found it, and a call to a function that can allocate still roots every live local and operand. The call graph is read from the same emitted text the safepoint decision uses, so an analysis answer cannot drift from the lowering it describes, and every call that can collect keeps its roots.
- [QBE-ROOTINLINE-1] Generated code links and restores the root chain with straight-line IL instead of calling `myl_native_root_push` and `myl_qbe_root_restore`: at each safepoint it loads the heap's root head once through the frozen offset of `myl_native_heap.roots`, then per live root stores the previous head, the traced address and the type descriptor into that root's four-word slot and links the slot into that same head field, and the restore stores the frame's mark back into it. The slot layout matches `struct myl_native_root` field for field, the offset is frozen by a matching `_Static_assert` in `src/qbe_runtime.c`, and the two runtime helpers remain for the C side even though no generated call site names them, so roots, collection timing and results are unchanged.
- [QBE-ROOTS-2] A live root whose value is one word is traced through a copy taken at the push instead of the address of the slot holding it: the push reads the local or operand slot into the root record's fourth word and points the traced address at that word, so no root push takes a slot's address and QBE is free to keep a scalar in a register across a safepoint. An aggregate root, whose traced address is storage the collector walks rather than a value it reads, keeps the address form, and clearing a traced one-word local is a store rather than a `memset` call, which would take the address too. The collector is unchanged and still reads the traced pointer through `address`; a copy taken at the push is exact for the whole safepoint because collection is non-moving and stop-the-world at the allocation that pushed it, so values, traps, traces, collection timing and results are unchanged.
- [QBE-ALIAS-CLASS-1] Emitted QBE IL marks every address the frontend knows names memory outside the running function's stack frame with a `# myl-obj %temp` comment line on its own: the receiver of a managed container's length, capacity, iteration and element operations, the storage word an element read loads, and the backing pointer the inline list and byte-buffer append paths write through. QBE's lexer drops a `#` comment to end of line, so its assembly is byte-identical with and without the markers; the LLVM adapter reads them, together with the `alloc4/8/16` slots of the same unit, to give each access scoped alias metadata saying that this activation's stack storage and the managed heap are disjoint, which is what lets a loop keep a container's length and storage pointer in registers instead of reloading the header on every element. An address neither side places carries no metadata and keeps aliasing everything.
- [QBE-APPEND-FAST-1] A list `append` running in a frame that keeps the root protocol lowers the capacity-sufficient case inline: the generated code loads the receiver's generation, compares `length` against `capacity`, and when the element fits stores it at `storage + length * stride` and bumps `length` and `generation` without calling anything. Only a full receiver reaches a grow helper, and the frame's live roots are linked around that call alone, so the common path allocates nothing, publishes no root, and no longer takes the value slot's address. A one-word scalar element is read into a register once and carried through both paths by value — the grow call is `myl_native_list_append_scalar(heap, list, value)`, which copies the low `element->size` little-endian bytes — while a wider or aggregate element keeps the pointer `myl_native_list_append` copies from. The frozen-receiver sentinel the helper tests for cannot occur on a list — the runtime only increments the generation and an image-frozen literal carries generation zero — so the refusal check stays with the helper on the path that can still reach it. The stride is the emitter's static element layout, the same one the indexed read and write paths use and the same `element->size` the runtime copies, and values, traps, roots and collection timing are unchanged.
- [QBE-BUFFER-APPEND-1] A `byte_buffer` append running in a frame that keeps the root protocol lowers the capacity-sufficient case inline: `append_range` first repeats the three refusals `myl_native_bytes_buffer_append_range` performs before it writes — a negative `start`, an `end` before `start`, and an `end` past `len(data)` — then compares `length + count` against `capacity`, and when the octets fit copies them to `storage + length` through the non-allocating `myl_native_bytes_copy_octets` and bumps `length`; `append_byte` likewise checks `0 <= value <= 255`, compares `length` against `capacity`, stores the octet and bumps `length`. Only a refused request, an out-of-range octet or a full receiver reaches the runtime helper, and the frame's live roots are linked around that call alone, so the common path allocates nothing and publishes no root. The refusals come first because they are what make the copy's operands trustworthy: with them answered, `count` is bounded by the source's own length, `length + count` cannot wrap, and `length <= capacity`, which every constructor, grow and reserve maintains, makes `capacity - length` the room that is really there. The frozen-receiver refusal the helper performs cannot occur on this path: the checker admits the in-place append methods only on `byte_buffer`, whose sole constructor is `byte_buffer(...)`'s fresh heap allocation, so no image-frozen literal can be a receiver and the refusal stays with the helper on the path that can still reach it. Both inlined cases answer exactly what the helper answered — `True` when they wrote, `False` when the request was refused without writing, and the allocation trap when the budget refused a growth — and values, traps, roots and collection timing are unchanged.
- [LIST-GROWTH-1] A list's backing is grown in place once it is big enough to own a whole segment, by the same `myl_native_heap_extend` a byte buffer grows with: the elements already stored are the ones the copy would have rewritten and the pages already faulted are the ones the copy would have faulted a second time for. Nothing outside the list holds a pointer into that backing — every element read, indexed write, sort, slice and dictionary entry derives its address from the header on the use that needs it — so the one value that cannot survive a move is an element pointer already inside the backing, which is exactly the source `myl_native_list_extend(list, list)` passes, and that value keeps the ordinary allocate-and-copy path. A backing the allocator cannot extend, an arena allocation, an immortal block and a request the budget refuses all fall back the same way, so values, lengths, capacities, identity, traps, traces and collection timing are unchanged.
- [DICT-GETORPUT-1] A dictionary remembers where its last string-keyed probe landed and which key object it landed on, so the second half of a read-modify-write — `counts[k] = counts.get(k, 0) + 1` probes one key object twice in a row — answers with two pointer comparisons instead of another payload hash, table walk and content comparison. The record is a hint and never an answer: it is accepted only when the queried text pointer is the recorded one *and* the live entry at the recorded position still holds that same pointer, which identity proves to be the queried key, so an insertion, a `pop` that shifts every later entry down, a `clear`, a rebuilt index or a different text object with equal content each fall back to the full probe and reach the entry that probe reaches. A string key's miss is never recorded — a miss stays a miss until a write makes it a hit, which a stale record could not be allowed to shadow; [PY-DICT-4] extends the record to keys compared by value. Lookups, insertion order, defaults, traps and arena ownership are unchanged.
- [QBE-PURE-READ-1] The set of runtime callees a lowering may name without becoming a safepoint covers every read-only collection surface: a dictionary probe (`read`, `contains`, `length`, `key_at`, `value_at`), a byte-string search (`find`, `find_any`, `starts_with`, `ends_with`, `has_byte`) and the octet copy an inlined byte-buffer append performs all take a `const` receiver, walk storage the caller already holds, and allocate nothing, so the one write any of them makes — the payload hash a text caches in its own header — never reaches a collection. An instruction whose body names only these pushes no root, and a frame whose every call is one of them keeps no root protocol at all, exactly as `QBE-SAFEPOINT-1` and `QBE-PURECALL-1` already treat the numeric and text readers. Values, traps, traces, collection timing and results are unchanged.
- [BUFFER-GROWTH-1] A byte buffer's backing is grown in place once it is big enough to own a whole segment: `myl_native_heap_extend` hands the block's own reservation back to the host to resize, so the octets already written are the ones the copy would have rewritten and the pages already faulted are the ones the copy would have faulted a second time for. It answers NULL — leaving the block untouched — for a payload that is not the whole of its own one-slot segment (a size-class slot, an arena allocation, an immortal image block), for a count that is not larger, for one that would overflow, and for one the memory budget refuses after the same collection the ordinary allocation path runs; the caller then allocates a fresh block and copies. The block's element count and descriptor are rewritten exactly as `myl_native_heap_alloc` writes them, the heap is charged only the added stride, and the segment is relinked if the reservation moved. The bytes the block did not already hold are zeroed unless its element descriptor is one the collector never interprets — `MYL_NATIVE_PLAIN` or `MYL_NATIVE_TRACE_NONE`, exactly the test that keeps the tracing drain loop and `trace` off the payload — so an uninitialized tail can never be followed as a reference. A `byte_buffer` is the only grower: its backing is private, because `byte_buffer(...)` builds a fresh allocation or a copy and `view` refuses a mutable receiver, so no other reference can observe a moved reservation. Octets, lengths, capacities, identities, traps, traces and collection timing are unchanged.
- [QBE-PROMOTE-1] A function declares its scalar local and operand slots in the entry block, before any label the frame protocol introduces, because QBE promotes a slot to a temporary only when its `alloc8` sits there; per-pc aggregate scratch stays after the protocol so a zero-length aggregate is never folded to a null load, and a slot declaration no instruction reads is removed after emission. Observable behavior — values, traps, traces and exit status — is unchanged; the generated code calls no runtime helper and only promotes what QBE can prove is a uniform load/store slot.
- [QBE-PROMOTE-2] Which slot declarations a unit may drop is decided by one whole-unit pass that counts every `%name` reference for each name the unit defines as a slot, instead of rescanning the whole unit once per definition, so rewriting a unit costs its own length rather than its length multiplied by its slot count. The count is exactly the one the per-name scan reported: a reference is a `%` immediately followed by that name as a complete identifier run, so `%l1` is neither counted as nor dropped on account of `%l10`, and a unit whose text merely contains a name without a preceding `%` still counts nothing. The unit text the pass produces, and therefore every emitted unit, is byte-identical.
- [QBE-FRAMES-1] Each generated guest function owns one frame record in its own native stack — `prev`, its function metadata and its position — links it with two stores and unlinks it on every return path, so entering and leaving a frame is straight-line code rather than two runtime calls. The prologue enforces the logical `--max-frames` ceiling and the execution stack floor with inline compares whose cold path raises `R_FRAME_LIMIT` through `myl_qbe_fail_frame`. Trap traces are the `prev` chain innermost-first, `omitted_frames` still counts the depth beyond the first sixteen, and `--max-frames`, trace text, and the bounded guest stack are unchanged.
- [QBE-POSITION-1] A build without step accounting stores a frame position only where a diagnostic can read it: an instruction whose body can reach a failure path, an `assert`, or a call site keeps its `storel <pc>, %position`, and every instruction that cannot fail and calls nothing omits it, leaving the previous observed position in place. Trap traces, `assert` messages and their source locations are unchanged; a build with step accounting keeps every position because every instruction can then exhaust the budget.
- [QBE-STICKY-1] The sticky-trap test after an instruction is emitted only when that instruction's own body can leave `ctx->trap` set and still fall through: a guest call and a runtime helper that reports failure by return value can, while a body naming only `myl_qbe_fail_*` helpers — every one of which unwinds immediately — or whose every call is followed by the `jmp @failure` that unwinds it cannot, so a widening cast, an inline range check and an inline overflow check carry no `loadl %trap`. A trap raised anywhere still stops the program at the same instruction with the same code, trace and detail, and an opcode outside the tested set keeps its test unconditionally.
- [QBE-UNWIND-1] In a build that dropped both step and frame accounting, a trap is no longer reported through the sticky `ctx->trap` flag tested after every call: the guest-stack trampoline arms a `setjmp` before the entry runs, and the first failing `myl_qbe_fail_*` helper builds the trace on the generated stack and leaves through `longjmp` to that point, so no guest instruction runs after the failure. The per-call `loadl %trap` test disappears, while a position store stays at every call site because a callee's trace names the caller's pc; a build with step or frame accounting keeps the flag test and the sticky path, and the direct C entry points and budget parsing keep the sticky behavior because no jump is armed for them.
- [QBE-DECODE-RANGE-1] `src[start:end].decode()` lowers to one range decoder instead of a slice followed by a decode: when the slice's value is stored into a temporary whose only reader is the `.decode` two instructions later and no branch enters either instruction, the slice call, its copy and its allocation disappear and the decode validates and widens the clamped range in place, leaving a null byte string in the slot the slice would have filled so a root push over that slot still traces a value. Endpoint clamping, invalid-UTF-8 results and every produced value are unchanged; a refused allocation is reported at the decode instead of the slice, and a temporary read anywhere else keeps the slice.
- [QBE-TEARDOWN-1] A ReleaseFast executable's single guest run is also the process's last: its generated entry declares that before the run, and the runner then leaves the managed heap to the process instead of freeing every live object first. A Debug executable keeps that sweep as a heap check and a library entry never claims it, so a host that runs the guest repeatedly still reclaims each run's heap; traps, exit statuses, output, `--gc-stress`, the collector's own sweeps of unreachable objects and every other observable behavior are unchanged, and only the final walk over objects that were still live disappears.
- [QBE-BULK-TEXT-1] Raw top-level text is encoded into the line buffer in one pass: `myl_print_text` at depth zero sizes the value's UTF-8 exactly, reserves that many bytes through the same doubling grower `myl_print_raw` uses, and fills the buffer directly, so each scalar costs a width test and a store instead of a call and a capacity test. Nested text keeps the repr-quoting and escaping loop unchanged, an empty value writes nothing, a line that cannot grow is marked failed exactly as a refused `realloc` marked it, and the bytes written, the renderer table the compiler emits and `stream.write`'s returned scalar count are unchanged.
- [QBE-TEXT-BLOCK-1] A `str` built at run time keeps its UTF-8 payload in the same managed block as its header: 24 bytes of header — the Unicode scalar count at offset 0, the payload's octet count, and a flag word — followed by one octet per scalar of source text, one allocation whose object payload holds no traced pointer, so the collector never walks it and one free releases both. A text therefore costs its own octets plus one header instead of four bytes per scalar plus a list header, an owned text's payload address is the header's tail (a view reads another text's payload, [TEXT-VIEW-1]), and a static literal is the same header and payload laid out in the image.
- [UTF8-TEXT-1] A `str` stores validated UTF-8 octets with a cached Unicode scalar count and an "every scalar is below 0x80" flag, so `len` and equality are one compare and every boundary that already holds UTF-8 — `from_utf8`, `utf8`, `bytes.encode`, a file path, a print sink — copies octets instead of decoding and re-encoding scalars. Scalar indexing, slicing, a stepped slice and `ord` keep Python's scalar semantics by computing byte offsets on demand: a scalar index is a byte index on ASCII text and one payload walk otherwise, and the same walk turns a byte offset back into a scalar index for `find`. Where scalars were compared, octets are: equality is a byte compare, order is UTF-8 byte order (which is code point order), `find`/`count`/`split`/`replace`/`splitlines`/`split()` scan octets because an ASCII scalar is its own octet and no octet of a non-ASCII scalar is below 0x80, case mapping and the three classifications stay ASCII-only, and rendering a nested text escapes per octet. A stepped slice gathers scalars, so it walks the payload once and copies the selected scalars' own octets rather than reading a stride of it, and the arena escape copier clones a text as one payload copy in the enclosing region because there is no pointer in it to patch. A whole-path file helper borrows a payload only for the write helpers that consume one, because `open`'s third operand is its mode spelling and the request's text bit describes the handle that mode names, not that operand. Immutability, UTF-8 validity, the `R_TEXT_SCALAR`, `R_TEXT_BOUNDS` and `R_EMPTY_SEPARATOR` traps, `dict[str, ...]` keys and every produced value are unchanged, and a text built from scalars outside `0..0x10FFFF` or inside the surrogate range is refused rather than stored. The header's fourth word is a payload-hash cache a dictionary fills on its first lookup of that text and no payload write can invalidate, so a read-modify-write of one key text hashes its octets once rather than once per lookup.
- [QBE-POSITION-2] In a build without step accounting the remaining position stores sit on the cold path: an instruction whose only trap route is its own `myl_qbe_fail_*` call stores its pc immediately before that call instead of at the top of its body, so a loop body carries no store, while an instruction naming any other call keeps its store at the top because a callee, a runtime helper or a provider can trap on its own and the trace then reads this frame's position. Every pc a trace, an `assert` message or a frame-limit report can name is the pc that store named before, and a build with step accounting keeps every per-instruction store because the budget check can fail at any instruction.
- [QBE-STEP-ACCOUNTING-1] Instruction step accounting is build policy: a Debug build, a shared library, and a `run` that named `--max-steps=N` keep the exact per-instruction position marker and step check, while a ReleaseFast executable whose build arguments name no ceiling omits the budget check and its `max_steps` metadata (keeping the per-instruction position marker that trap traces and `assert` messages read), so `mylang run` without `--max-steps` and `mylang build --mode ReleaseFast` compile straight-line code. Such a binary reports `R_NO_STEP_ACCOUNTING`, naming how to get a bounded run, when the runtime is given `--max-steps=`, instead of silently ignoring the ceiling; `--max-frames`, `--timeout` and `--max-memory` stay runtime-enforced and unaffected.
- [QBE-UNCHECKED-1] Safety code is build policy, selected once per program and never by default: `mylang build --unchecked` (and `mylang run --unchecked`, and the emitters' internal `--drop-safety-checks`) omits the integer-overflow guards on add, subtract, multiply and unary negate, the narrowing integer-cast guards, and the index-bounds guards on lists, fixed arrays, `bytes`, `str`, dictionary position reads and `pop`, keeping in every case the value computation the fast path uses — the sum, the product, the normalized index, the element address, the runtime call — and dropping only the test and its cold failure block. When such a build also carries neither an instruction nor a frame ceiling it further omits the per-call 32-byte frame record, its link into `ctx->active_frame`, the stack-floor test, the function-metadata store and every `%position` store, so no generated code touches the frame chain at all. Allocation failure, the memory budget, GC roots and everything the collector traces, host-call and capability validation, `assert`, arena escape checks, shift-count range tests, dictionary missing-key and list concurrent-modification errors, and the division-by-zero and signed `MIN // -1` tests that guard a hardware fault are all still emitted. A would-be trap is undefined behaviour under this policy, and a trap that does still fire reports no frames and no `assert` message text. The policy is refused where it cannot be honored — `mylang test`, `--mode Debug` and `--library` — while a build that named `--max-steps`/`--max-frames`/`--step-accounting` keeps the frame protocol and the accounting that enforces the ceiling and gives up only the value guards, and an unbounded unchecked binary given a runtime ceiling still reports `R_NO_STEP_ACCOUNTING`/`R_NO_FRAME_ACCOUNTING`. A build that does not name the policy emits byte-identical IL to one from a compiler without it.
- [QBE-BLOCKS-1] Straight-line instructions share one basic block: an instruction falls through into its successor's body, and an instruction label is emitted only when a branch names it, so locals cross instruction boundaries in registers instead of round-tripping through their slots. Step accounting, trap positions, program results and every other observable behavior are unchanged.
- [QBE-INTOPS-1] Checked integer multiplication, truncating and flooring division and remainder, shifts, and unary negation lower to inline QBE instructions with the same cold trap paths the runtime helpers used: an overflow reports `myl_qbe_fail_overflow_op` with the evaluated operands and width, division by zero reports `myl_qbe_fail_division`, and an out-of-range shift count or a negated minimum still calls the runtime helper, so trap codes, trap text, structured detail, sticky first-trap behavior and results are byte-identical to the call-based lowering.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-1] A NaN is canonicalized when it is observed, not when it is produced. Binary64 `+`, `-`, `*` and `/` lower to the bare QBE operation and a cast back to the value's bit word, with no unordered self-compare, branch or call beside it; the unary helpers (`sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc`, `abs`, negation) and a foreign function's `float` result likewise keep the bits IEEE and the hardware produced, so a NaN may carry any payload and sign between operations. No guest spelling reads a float's representation — `float` is a private builtin domain and the raw `f64_*` spellings are removed — so the only places a payload could show are the observation points below, and each of them sees the canonical quiet NaN `0x7ff8000000000000` or treats every NaN alike. Arithmetic and comparisons propagate NaNs as IEEE does and every comparison with a NaN is false. A NaN folded at compile time — by the compiler's constant evaluator, QBE or LLVM — is under the same rule: its bits may differ from the running processor's, and no observation point can tell.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-2] Text observes no NaN payload: `str`, `stream.print`, f-string interpolation, container rendering and `byte_buffer.append_float` spell every NaN `nan`, whatever its sign or payload, never `-nan`.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-3] A `float` argument crosses into an `extern def` as the canonical quiet NaN when it is any NaN: the generated C ABI wrapper selects the canonical word with straight-line mask arithmetic before the call, so foreign code that reads a double's sign or payload (`copysign`, `signbit`, a byte copy) sees `0x7ff8000000000000`. A NaN a foreign function returns enters the guest unchanged, like any produced NaN.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-4] Equality never compares a float's bits: `==`, `!=` and `in` over lists, records, fixed arrays, options and unions holding floats compare each float numerically, so two NaNs are unequal whatever their payloads and signs, and positive and negative zero are equal.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-5] Sorting observes no NaN payload: `list.sort()` and `sorted` order every NaN after the ordered floats, keep NaNs of any sign or payload in their input order among themselves, and print them as `nan`.
- [FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-6] A `float` copied to a `host def` callback crosses as the canonical quiet NaN when it is any NaN: the host metadata marks the builtin binary64 domain with a 64-bit width, the only domain that carries one, and the copied-value bridge rewrites that domain's backing word to `0x7ff8000000000000` when it holds any NaN, at any depth inside records, arrays and variants. Finite floats, infinities and a user integer domain whose word happens to look like a NaN cross unchanged, and a NaN a host returns enters the guest unchanged. The packed and scalar fast paths never carry a float, and wire, codec, typed-JSON and `invoke` boundaries reject `float`, so these are the only host exits a float's bits have.
- [QBE-FLOAT-ENV-1] The process floating-point environment is verified once, before guest code runs and again after every C FFI call returns, instead of on every floating operation: binary64 arithmetic and comparisons lower to bare inline QBE `add`/`sub`/`mul`/`div`/`ceqd`/`cltd`/`cled`, and the unary and conversion helpers no longer re-probe the environment. A context whose rounding mode is not round-to-nearest, or whose subnormal product behavior differs, traps `R_FLOAT_ENVIRONMENT` before the guest entry point executes, and a foreign call that changes the environment re-arms the same sticky trap before its result is used, so trap codes, trap text, sticky first-trap behavior and every result bit are unchanged.
- [QBE-LITERAL-1] A `str` or `bytes` literal whose every element is a constant scalar push is published once as an immortal image object instead of being constructed on every evaluation: the managed lowering emits a marker carrying the element descriptor, width, count and native payload bytes, the metadata unit turns each distinct marker into one C data block holding the managed object header, the list header and the element storage the runtime constructor would have written, and the lowered code loads the shared payload pointer. The data symbol hashes the element descriptor and payload, so equal literals share one object across every unit. Frozen objects carry the immortal header flag and arena region 0: the collector never queues, re-traces, frees or bulk-releases them, explicit release refuses them, and arena ownership checks treat them as heap-owned, so they survive `--gc-stress`, `with arena()` scopes and teardown. Content, type, length, indexing and equality are byte-identical to a constructed value, and the lowered body names no allocating callee, so a function whose only allocatable opcode is such a literal pushes no roots.
- [QBE-ELEMENT-READ-1] A list or byte-string element read whose element type the schema fixes as a plain scalar — an integer, `bool`, or a nominal domain over one of those, which includes `float` — lowers to one bounds-checked load from the receiver's backing storage instead of a call to the generic element-copy routine and the aggregate scratch it fills. The length comes from the list header, the index the read already carries is compared against it unsigned, the cold path raises `R_LIST_BOUNDS` through the helper that recovers an original index from an already-normalized one with the same index and length, and the live path addresses `storage + index * stride` from the header's storage word and loads the element with the width and signedness of its backing type (`loadsb`, `loadub`, `loadsh`, `loaduh`, `loadsw`, `loaduw` or `loadl`). Negative indices, the trap code, message, location and structured detail, and every value produced are unchanged, and aggregate elements follow the checked value-snapshot and projection rules of `QBE-AGGREGATE-READ-1`.
- [QBE-AGGREGATE-READ-1] A list read with a concrete nonzero native element layout checks the same normalized index and copies that exact layout directly into its value snapshot, without a generic runtime element-copy call. A record consumed only by an uninterrupted chain of field reads ending in a scalar may instead lend its checked backing address to that chain: no branch enters it, no allocation, call or mutation intervenes, and no interior address escapes. Records with release obligations and records retained as values keep snapshots, and a record returned straight from the read takes that snapshot in the return copy itself (`QBE-RETFWD-1`); zero-sized elements keep the existing runtime path. Managed fields remain rooted when later operations can collect, list mutation and growth cannot alter a retained value, and negative-index behavior, bounds diagnostics, step checks, trap positions and arena ownership remain unchanged.
- [QBE-ELEMENT-ADDRESS-1] A `for x in xs` iteration local that owns nothing names its element where it lives instead of copying it out of the list: when the element read is followed straight away by the store into a local of the element's own type, that store is the local's only write, every read of the local lies in the branch-free run that follows it, and no instruction in that run can call, allocate, release or mutate — so the backing storage cannot move, be freed or be written before the last read, and the next iteration overwrites the address before anything reads it again — the store keeps the checked address the read produced and every read of the local reaches the element itself. The local's slot becomes one word, the aggregate copy the loop body used to pay disappears, and every value the body computes, every trap, its code, message, location and structured detail, and the mutation check's behavior are unchanged.
- [QBE-AGGREF-2] An aggregate local read as an operand is named by its own slot even when later operands are pushed above it: the borrow reaches the instruction that consumes the operand — the first one whose execution leaves the stack at or below that operand — instead of stopping at the next instruction, so `f(record, x)` passes the record's address rather than snapshotting it into aggregate scratch first. The window is only entered when every instruction in it leaves the stack no lower, no instruction in it writes that local (a store, move, drop or match binding declines the borrow), no branch reaches into it without passing through the read, and the type owns nothing and is not zero-sized, so the address is still the value and every produced value is the value the snapshot produced.
- [QBE-MATCHPROJ-1] A match arm whose payload binding owns nothing reads the payload where it already lives instead of copying it out of the scrutinee: when the scrutinee is the read-only local the dispatch loads and no branch reaches the dispatch directly, the union's payload address is the scrutinee local plus the union's payload offset, a compile-time constant, so it is stored into the binding once at entry and every arm reads fields, tags and elements through it. The binding is never written by anything but its own arm, the scrutinee local is never written (a whole-value store, move, drop or second binding all decline the projection, and a record binding is immutable so a field write through it is already rejected), and a payload that owns nothing needs no copy to release. Root pushing, drop cleanup and the by-address call convention already read such a local through its address, so a payload containing managed fields is traced where it lives, no ownership moves, and every value an arm computes is the value the copy produced.
- [QBE-AGGREF-1] An unmanaged aggregate argument crosses a call boundary as the address of the caller's storage instead of a second copy of its bytes: a parameter the callee never writes is read through that address, so neither the argument copy the by-value boundary made on each side nor a snapshot of the whole record per use is emitted, while a parameter the body stores, moves, drops or binds a match payload into keeps a private slot and its entry copy, so a write inside a callee can never reach the caller's record. A parameter whose type releases something — a `Result[TextFile, E]`, whose handle contributes a `drop` without the move-only layout that would forbid copying — a move-only aggregate, and a zero-length aggregate all keep the by-value boundary, the entry copy and the release of the callee's own copy exactly as before. An ordinary local whose type releases nothing and whose value the very next instruction alone consumes — one that pops it, overwrites its operand slot with its own result, or reads it as an argument — is named by its own slot the same way, one copy fewer on every field, tag or element read, and a match arm that would bind a payload into that same slot keeps the copy so no region is copied onto itself. Which parameters cross by address follows from the parameter's type and the body's writes alone, so direct calls, function values, callbacks, host wrappers, exported signatures and instantiated generic bodies agree; `mutates(...)` enforcement, arena and managed ownership, `--gc-stress`, the element read of `QBE-ELEMENT-READ-1` and every trap code, message and location are unchanged, a callee that traps leaves each caller local holding what it held before, and every produced value is the value the copy produced.
- [QBE-CTORFWD-1] A record constructor whose value the very next instruction stores into a local builds the record in that local's own storage instead of the per-pc aggregate scratch — the same field stores in the same order, after every operand was already evaluated into its slot — so the whole-record copy and the now-dead scratch declaration disappear, and the destination is claimed only when it owns nothing (so no release of its old value has to happen before the new one lands), is this frame's own nonzero-sized value of the constructor's type that the constructor fills without allocating, and is read by no instruction that can reach the constructor and has no branch entering the store itself (so no live operand can name the storage being filled and the store only ever runs over the record the constructor just built), leaving every value, trap, trace, root push and exit status unchanged.
- [QBE-1] Emits deterministic independent QBE native function units with stable symbols and body-local instructions; unrelated body edits and declaration reordering preserve unchanged units. Unsupported native features fail before publishing output.
- [QBE-2] Builds Linux x86-64 executables and shared libraries directly through the Zig CLI without Python or a compiler source checkout; bundled runtime inputs, verified object reuse, source diagnostics, and native API adapters preserve native build behavior. The generated entry adapter and each guest function are separately cached objects, the fixed runtime translation units are archived once per build environment and linked after every object that can reference them, and section-level collection drops runtime a program never reaches. A stored object or archive is reused only when a fingerprint over its length and contents matches, so damaged bytes or a damaged fingerprint rebuild that one entry; an unchanged executable is republished by comparing length and then bytes, retaining its inode and modification time. `--run` executes a temporary native binary with inherited I/O and propagated status, then removes it while retaining cached objects. Shared-library links also bind the library's own data-descriptor symbols internally, so a guest object's non-PIC references resolve within the library instead of failing as preemptible relocations.
- [QBE-FRAGMENT-REUSE-1] A project emission keys each function's QBE unit by its emission inputs — its lowered body, the signature, policy and parameter locals of every function it names, the full schema of every record it can reach, the whole-program switches, and for the second pass each named function's allocation and parameter-write facts — with record and function ordinals written as their names and source positions left out, and answers an unchanged key from a fragment store beside the frontend cache instead of lowering the unit again; the reused manifest is byte-identical to a fresh emission after body, signature, interface and record edits, a stored text that is missing is a miss, and under the internal `--lean-manifest` (which `build --project` passes, and which `run`, `run-project` and the native-test session always use) the native build receives each unit's digest, stored path and marker lines instead of its text, names the object by that digest, and reads the text only to build a missing object; `work-report-v1` counts a unit answered from the store as a reused, not an emitted, fragment, a cached run manifest naming a stored text that is gone is a miss, and a reused unit's operand stacks are analysed again only when it converts an aggregate to text, the one case whose renderer they name.
- [EMIT-REPLAY-1] A whole-project `emit-project-qbe --lean-manifest` remembers its rendered warnings and manifest in the compiler identity's fragment store, one slot per entry path, emission switches and diagnostic format holding only that configuration's latest emission, keyed by the loaded modules' names, paths and bytes and the configured policy identity; an identical later request replays both after loading the graph, with nothing checked, lowered or emitted, and any changed input, another compiler identity, or a named unit text missing from the store is a miss.
- [BUILD-NOOP-1] After a successful native build the driver records beside the output a receipt keyed by the frontend manifest, the build environment and driver generation, the artifact kind, mode, target, output path and every link argument, together with the output's inode, size and modification time; a later build with the same key and an untouched output returns without parsing the manifest, staging objects or linking, while `--run`, `--report`, a provider or explicit link inputs never take the shortcut.
- [NATIVE-BUILD-RECLAIM-1] A native build owns its filesystem and process scratch in a reclaiming arena freed when the build ends, over the general allocator rather than the process arena, so a long-lived host that rebuilds its guest on every reload does not accumulate build memory.
- [NATIVE-STAGING-1] Every native build stages private scratch beneath one hidden `.pending/` folder in its destination directory and removes only its own child plus that folder when empty, so concurrent builders and interrupted runs leave at most one contained folder instead of one `.pending-<hex>` sibling per invocation.
- [NATIVE-STAGING-2] Staging creation treats a shared `.pending/` folder a concurrent reclaim deleted as a lost race rather than a host failure. One creation can lose two windows — the folder may vanish between the `mkdir` and the existence check that tolerates it, and again before the private child is created — so each attempt recreates the folder and its child under a bounded budget and reports the last real filesystem error when the budget is spent, never a synthetic not-found. Parallel test children and documentation examples therefore stop dropping work with a spurious `unexpected errno: 2`.
- [NATIVE-SCRATCH-ROOT-1] Host scratch that has no destination directory of its own — the materialized source snapshot every `mylang test` child stages — lives beneath `$TMPDIR` when that variable is set and non-empty, read from the command's environment map, and beneath `/tmp` otherwise, so a host whose `/tmp` is a small or quota-bound tmpfs can move guest test staging onto disk instead of failing every test with `DiskQuota`.
- [NATIVE-SCRATCH-ROOT-2] End to end, `mylang test` run with `TMPDIR` naming a directory stages every compiler child's materialized snapshot beneath `$TMPDIR/.pending/` and nothing beneath `/tmp/.pending/`, and removes that staging when it finishes, whether the tests passed or the project was rejected before any test ran; with `TMPDIR` exported empty it stages beneath `/tmp/.pending/`.
- [NATIVE-SCRATCH-OWNER-1] A `mylang test` session's scratch directory, and a directory run's shared report workspace, are *owned* scratch: the creating process holds an exclusive advisory lock on a `.owner` marker (mode 0600) inside it until it removes the directory, and releases that lock only after the tree is gone. Creating owned scratch first reclaims every sibling under the same `.pending/` folder whose marker it can lock exclusively — the kernel releases the lock when its holder exits in any way, so only scratch a killed or crashed run abandoned qualifies. A sibling with no marker, a marker another process (or this one) still holds, a marker this user cannot open, and any non-directory entry are left alone; no age or wall clock is consulted. A fresh directory whose marker a concurrent sweep took before it was locked is abandoned to that sweep and a new one is staged under a bounded budget, and a filesystem without advisory locks yields an ordinary unmarked directory.
- [NATIVE-LAYOUT-MEMO-1] A builtin type's ABI layout fingerprint is computed at most once per catalog. The fingerprint of a builtin depends only on its type tag — the layout switch reads no catalog state and the digest recurses only through an aggregate's fields, of which a builtin has none — so a memoized digest can never disagree with a freshly computed one for the same tag, which a test pins for every builtin tag and for an aggregate laid out both before and after the memo is warm. A memoized digest is used only where a digest was already computed from the same tag; aggregate layouts keep their existing per-record cache and their own recursion, and a tag outside the builtin range is still digested directly.
- [QBE-HOST-COMPILER-1] A native build emits its guest's QBE units through its own executable by default (`/proc/self/exe emit-project-qbe`); `MYLANG_HOST_COMPILER` overrides that frontend with an explicit compiler, which a host application driven from a binary that is not itself a compiler supplies. `MYLANG_QBE` and `MYLANG_ZIG` likewise name the native tools when neither a flag, a tool beside the compiler, nor `PATH` locates them.
- [QBE-TRAP-DETAIL-1] Native traps carry a bounded, versioned structured detail captured from values the failing operation already evaluated: original index and receiver length for bounds failures (negative indexes preserved), and evaluated operands/width for division and checked overflow. Text and structured JSON render from the same detail; the JSON path does not reparse text. Native children emit one bounded `\x1eMYLANG-TRAP ` + JSON record on stderr while ordinary stdout, diagnostics, exit statuses, trap codes, locations, call stacks, omitted-frame counts and first-trap stickiness are preserved, and budget/host failures remain detail-free.
- [TRAP-UNSIGNED-1] An arithmetic trap's operands are quoted in the operation's own signedness: an unsigned overflow (`add`, `sub`, `mul`, a range-checked narrow width), an unsigned division by zero, a cast from an unsigned source and an unsigned negation render `left`/`right` as unsigned decimals (`(add: left 18446744073709551614, right 5, width 64)` where it used to read `left -2`), in the human diagnostic, in the `\x1eMYLANG-TRAP` record (which adds `"unsigned":true`) and in `--json`'s `trap_detail`. The generated cold call marks unsigned operands by OR-ing `MYL_QBE_UNSIGNED_OPERANDS` (0x100) into its width (overflow, cast) or operation (division) argument, and the runtime helpers set the same present bit from their `is_signed` argument; a signed operation's call, IL and text are byte-identical to before.

- [QBE-3] The compiler bundles only the native runtime build inputs — its runtime C sources and headers and the Zig runtime units it compiles, with their reference closure — never compiler tests or guest fixtures. A build materializes that tree once per build-time content hash, staging into a private directory and renaming so concurrent builds sharing a location never observe a partial tree, and reuses it on every later build. An explicit `--cache-dir` keeps the tree hermetically under `<cache-dir>/runtime/<hash>`; a default build instead shares a user-level location (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME/mylang/runtime/<hash>`, else `$HOME/.cache/mylang/runtime/<hash>`) so a fresh project reuses a tree another project already derived; it uses the per-project cache when neither variable names an absolute home, and also falls back to the per-project cache when the shared location cannot be written (for example a read-only home directory).
- [QBE-RUNTIME-IDENTITY-1] The compiler's build-time identities — the runtime `content_hash`, `compiler_identity` and `driver_identity` — are computed during `zig build`'s make phase, by the same run that copies the bundled runtime sources, from exactly those bytes. That run's cache key covers the absolute source directory and the contents of every production source, so an edit to one of them, or a second source tree, can never reuse another build's digests. The configure phase reads no source contents. Zig caches it without regard to the build root, so when two trees share one Zig cache the build fails and names the tree the reused configuration belongs to, instead of building with that tree's absolute paths. The bundled runtime equals the tree's `src/<path>` files byte for byte.
- [QBE-RUNTIME-INSTALLED-1] `zig build` prebuilds the runtime archive and text-formatter object for each optimize mode and installs them beside the compiler under `lib/mylang-runtime`, each named by a portable hash of the runtime content hash, pinned Zig version, mode and target. A `mylang build` links a matching installed artifact directly — skipping the runtime compile on a cold build — and reports `runtime_installed`; a removed, cross-target, mode-mismatched or corrupted install (its fingerprint no longer matching the `.digest` beside it) falls back to compiling the runtime from source. The portable hash excludes tool file stamps, so an installed archive matches a build that resolved its tools from a different path.
- [QBE-PARALLEL-MOVE-1] The vendored QBE register allocator builds a parallel move of any width: the move at a block edge carries one copy per phi of the successor plus one per temporary live into it, and the table that holds it grows on demand instead of stopping the build with `no more pm slots` past 64 copies, so a loop header carrying many phis — a dispatch loop whose managed locals are all live around it — compiles under `--mode Debug` and `--backend qbe` as it does under LLVM. This is the one local change to the vendored QBE 1.3 sources (`vendor/README.md`).
- [QBE-4] Native builds identify their inputs without rereading them: the compiler's own identity is a build-time digest of its sources, the Zig toolchain and the optimize mode, and external tools are identified by path, size and modification time. Object-cache keys still change when the native build driver, runtime, tools, target or mode change; an ordinary compiler edit that cannot change how emitted IL becomes an object does not move them (see `QBE-DRIVER-IDENTITY-1`). Build tools resolve from an explicit option, then beside the compiler's own executable, then `PATH`; the working directory never selects one.
- [BUILD-MODE-1] A native build that names no `--mode` builds `ReleaseFast`, because `mylang build` and `mylang run` are asked for a fast program; `--mode Debug` selects the unoptimized build explicitly and `--mode` still rejects any other spelling. The chosen mode is what the build usage text advertises, what `--report` states, and what the object cache, the runtime install and the run identity key on, so switching modes never reuses another mode's objects. A guest test run keeps its own `Debug` default, which `mylang test --mode ReleaseFast` overrides.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-1] A native build that names no backend builds with QBE in either optimize mode, because an edit-build loop wants the compile that takes a second rather than the LTO link that takes many; the LLVM release path is asked for by name, with `--release` or `--backend llvm`. An explicit backend is never changed: `--backend qbe` and `--qbe PATH` select QBE whatever the mode, and the LLVM-only controls (`--llvm-adapter`, `--llvm-opt`, `--lto`) are rejected until `--backend llvm` or `--release` names it. The build usage text says so, and `mylang run` and `mylang test` follow the same policy: their builds are QBE in either mode unless `--release` is given, and the test usage text says so.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-2] Backend resolution is the single place that turns an unnamed backend into a concrete tool: it resolves QBE in either mode, and the run cache names that same QBE whatever the mode (the mode keys a program through its own field). An LLVM build — `--release` or `--backend llvm` — resolves the qualified LLVM adapter and the exact Zig/Clang target CPU policy the link will use, and one that cannot find the installed adapter fails loudly with a tool error rather than silently building on QBE.
- [BUILD-RELEASE-1] `--release` on `mylang build` (and `build --run`), `run`, `run-project` and `test` is the shorthand for `--mode ReleaseFast --backend llvm --llvm-opt 3 --lto`, as `cargo build --release` is: the build receipt reports `"backend":"llvm","optimization":"3","lto":true`, an `--llvm-adapter` written after it still applies, and `--unchecked` is accepted with it. Written beside a control it already names (`--mode`, `--backend`, `--qbe`, `--llvm-opt`, `--lto`) in either order it is a usage error, not a silent override, and after `--` it belongs to the guest. A test run keys its receipts on it and hands it to each child of a directory run in place of `--mode`, and the link-input scan of `build` and `test` reads it and every other bare switch as taking no value, so a `--link FILE` written after one is still linked.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-5] An LLVM build that names no `--llvm-adapter` resolves one from `MYLANG_LLVM_ADAPTER` first and the copy installed beside the compiler under `lib/mylang-llvm/qbe_llvm.py` second, so a test binary that runs the compiler from a cache directory with no install beside it can still exercise `--release` by naming the adapter the way it names `qbe`. With neither source, resolution fails loudly and never falls back to another backend.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-3] The LLVM release path takes the cost out of a cold build without changing any object's identity: `zig build` prebuilds the runtime archive for each backend flavor (`qbe`, `llvm`, `llvm-lto`), keyed so a QBE archive can never be linked into an LLVM build, and a cold `mylang build` links the matching installed archive instead of recompiling the runtime. The guest units of one LLVM build are translated in concurrent waves — one adapter process per unit, up to the core count — after a single `--signatures-only` pass publishes the whole closure's call-site signatures, so every unit declares its callees from the same complete table a sequential build converges on. Each unit still lands as its own content-addressed object keyed exactly as before, a cache hit still starts no tool, and a wave reports the first tool failure only after reaping every child it started.
- [BUILD-BACKEND-4] An unchanged `mylang build` whose published executable still matches every input keeps it and relinks nothing, so a warm build does not pay the LTO link again. The decision is conservative: a `--provider`, `--link`, `--link-library` or `--library-path` disables it, and the executable, every cached object and the runtime archive must still be on disk. The sidecar beside the executable records a link identity over the object set, runtime archive, artifact kind, mode, target and symbol policy, and reuse requires an exact match.
- [QBE-SYMBOLS-1] A native build links its executable without a symbol table by default and keeps it when `--keep-symbols` is named, so a profile build can attribute `perf report` samples to guest functions while the shipping shape, its bytes and the reuse decision on an existing output are unchanged. The switch is a build control, not a runtime one: it is rejected with a value, is absent from `--run` argument forwarding, and changes no generated code.
- [QBE-RUNTIME-MERGE-1] The fixed runtime translation units compile into the archive's single member through one `zig build-obj` invocation naming every source, so a cold build pays one toolchain start rather than one per unit. The relocatable merge keeps each unit compiled with function and data sections, so the final `--gc-sections` link still drops runtime a program never reaches.
- [QBE-STRIP-1] Native output carries no debug information: every C compile and assembly passes `-g0`, the Zig runtime unit is built with `-fstrip`, and the link strips the symbol table, so a cached object, a runtime archive and a published executable hold only the code and data the program runs. The one exception is a build that asks for `--keep-symbols`, which keeps the symbol table so a profiler can name functions and still emits no debug information. Native traps, their frames and their structured detail come from the runtime's own tables, so a stripped program reports exactly what an unstripped one did.
- [QBE-GENERATION-1] A cache root keeps one live generation per artifact family: objects under `objects/<hash>`, `mylang run` programs under `run/<hash>` and the Zig test suites' guest executables under `exe/<hash>`, each named by the native driver identity and the bundled runtime hash. Tool stamps, mode and target stay in each entry's key but not in the generation, so every process of one compiler build shares one generation whatever path it resolved its tools from or mode it asked for, and never evicts a concurrent sibling's entries. Opening a generation removes every sibling generation in that family that no process still leases, so a changed driver or runtime never leaves its predecessors' artifacts behind; an existing generation is reused without a sweep, the shared staging folder is never swept, and eviction is best effort so a cache that cannot be swept still serves the generation it keeps.
- [NATIVE-GENERATION-LEASE-1] Every process that opens a generation directory for use holds a shared advisory lock on a `.lease` file inside it for the rest of its life, and a sweeper takes a non-blocking exclusive lock on each sibling's lease before evicting it and skips whatever it cannot take. Concurrent builds under one cache root — the compiler and the host applications, whose driver identities differ — therefore never delete a generation another process is still compiling or linking into. Leasing is advisory and best effort: an entry with no lease file is still swept, a filesystem that cannot lock behaves as before, and the kernel releases a dead process's lease, so no abandoned lease pins a generation and no wall clock is consulted.
- [NATIVE-PUBLISH-REPAIR-1] Publication into a generation heals a lost eviction race instead of failing it: a rename that reports the destination missing recreates and re-leases the generation directory and retries once, and a link whose cached input objects are no longer on disk rebuilds them into a restored generation and retries the link once. A link that fails with every input present is the program's own error and is reported unchanged.
- [QBE-DRIVER-IDENTITY-1] The object and executable caches key on a *native driver identity* — a build-time digest over the native build driver and link-policy sources (`src/qbe_build*.zig`, `src/native_link*.zig`) and the frame-map annotator that rewrites every unit's assembly (`src/qbe_frame_map.zig`), the Zig version and the optimize mode — rather than the whole compiler identity. Objects are already content addressed by the QBE IL the frontend emitted, the runtime sources by the bundled content hash, the tools by their stamps and the link flags by the cache contract, so a frontend or checker edit that cannot change how IL becomes an object no longer evicts every cached guest object and executable. The compiler identity is unchanged and still keys frontend/semantic caches and `mylang test` result reuse.
- [QBE-SPAWN-1] A native build starts every tool (QBE, the assembler, the C compiler, the linker, each LLVM adapter of a concurrent wave) with `clone(CLONE_VM | CLONE_VFORK)` instead of `fork`, so a start costs the same whatever the build process holds: the child resets every installed signal handler to the default, points standard input and output at `/dev/null` and standard error at the build's own or at a private pipe, restores the signal mask and executes the tool, named by path or found on its environment's `PATH`, in the environment the request names or, when it names none, this process's own (read back from `/proc/self/environ`), with `ZIG_PROGRESS` removed. A tool that cannot be executed is refused at start (`FileNotFound` for a missing one) with nothing left to reap; a reaped tool reports its exit status or signal and everything it wrote to standard error.
- [QBE-BATCH-1] The QBE arm compiles guest functions in batches. Units are ordered by module (the qualified name before its last `::`) and then symbol; a batch never spans two modules, starts at every symbol whose hash marks a boundary (one in 32 on average) and holds at most 128 functions (`MYLANG_QBE_BATCH=N` caps it, `1` compiles every function alone), and a unit that defines data compiles alone. One QBE run takes each member's IL as its own input file with every function placed in its own `.text.<symbol>` section, so `--gc-sections` still drops an unreferenced function; the assembly is frame-map annotated and assembled once, by Zig's bundled LLVM assembler invoked directly (`zig clang -cc1as`). The object is content addressed by the ordered keys of its members, so a changed function, driver, runtime, tool, mode or target misses exactly as a per-function object did, and an unchanged batch starts no tool. Missed batches build concurrently, as many at once as the cores the process may use and the machine's free memory at 256 MiB per tool allow; after the first failure no new batch starts, every running tool is still reaped and every scratch directory removed. `--report` still lists every function with its own key, now naming its batch's object, and adds `function_objects` and `compiled_function_objects`.
- [QBE-BATCH-2] A batched build and an unbatched one (`MYLANG_QBE_BATCH=1`) of the same program produce executables that behave identically — the same output, exit status and trap report — in a Debug build and in a frame-map (`--mode ReleaseFast --backend qbe`) build.
- [QBE-BATCH-3] A batched QBE build keeps each batch's function metadata — every function's `MylQbeFunction` record with its location, message and inline tables — in an object of its own, assembled from generated assembly that spells the same six-, three- and five-word records as the C unit (`qbe_runtime.h` asserts their sizes) and keyed by that text, so an edit that moves one module's lines reassembles that module's tables and nothing else. Each function's tables share one `.data.rel.ro.<symbol>_meta` section, so the final link keeps or drops them with the function, and every distinct string is stored once in a mergeable string section. The program-wide metadata C unit keeps the runtime descriptors, host bridges, literals, a declaration of every function's metadata and the frame-map roots; it and the entry adapter compile in the same concurrent pool as the batches.
- [BUILD-MEMORY-1] A batched QBE build stages each missed batch by reading, size-checking and writing one member's sectioned text at a time, removes a staged batch whose QBE never started, and reads the assembly it annotates and the object it fingerprints through a scratch arena freed after that step, so a cold build holds one batch's texts and outputs rather than the whole program's.
- [BUILD-POSITIONS-1] A lean manifest unit carries its per-instruction source positions as a `paths` table, one space-separated `path:line:column` entry per instruction in `positions`, and its assertion messages as `[pc, message]` pairs in `assertions`, instead of `locations` and `messages` (a unit with more than 256 distinct paths keeps the spelled-out form); the native build reads either spelling into the same location, message and function tables.
- [QBE-BUILD-READ-1] The native build reads every input file (a checked manifest, a cached object it verifies, a generated source) into one buffer of the length the file states, and reads it to its end the ordinary way only when that length is unknown or the file changed while being read; a missing file or a directory fails exactly as before. The object fingerprint beside every cached object and runtime archive is `mylang-object-v2:<length>:<Wyhash>`, because the pinned Zig's XxHash3 hashes long inputs at about 20 MB/s; a `v1` fingerprint never matches, so such an entry is rebuilt once.

- [EXPORT-PLACEMENT-1] A re-export `export module.Name;` naming an imported declaration may appear anywhere among top-level declarations after the module/import header. Discovery collects directives in source order with original locations; a duplicate or unknown re-export and private interface leaks still reject. The bare `export Name;` directive is removed and rejects with `E_EXPORT_REMOVED` (see `PUBLIC-DECL-3`). Directives inside function or type bodies reject, and formatting preserves directive order and comments.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-2] `list-declarations` reports a `public` declaration's `exported` flag as true while its `exports` array stays empty, and the declaration's byte range begins at the `public` modifier so a rename round-trips exactly.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-3] The bare `export NAME` directive is removed: it rejects with `E_EXPORT_REMOVED` at the directive, both in `list-declarations` and when a project is checked. Only the dotted `export module.Name` re-export, which publishes an imported declaration, remains an export directive.
- [REEXPORT-1] An export directive may name an imported declaration (`export shared.units.Distance`) as well as a local one. The facade module publishes that declaration under its own local name; a reference through the facade resolves to the defining module's identity with the target's own export privacy enforced, and interfaces, contracts and the project fingerprint report the facade name while the schema uses the defining declaration.
- [PUBLIC-DECL-1] A top-level declaration is published with a leading `public` modifier (`public def`, `public host def`, `public extern def`, `public record`, `public class`, `public enum`, `public union`, `public result`, `public domain`, `public resource`, `public type`, `public const`, `public constraint`). The modifier replaces the bare `export NAME` directive, so a module's published surface is written on the declarations themselves and `list-declarations` reports each declaration's export flag with an empty directive list.
- [REEXPORT-2] An export directive naming a qualified path whose alias is not imported, or whose target is not exported in its defining module, rejects with the existing located E_UNKNOWN_EXPORT/E_PRIVATE_SYMBOL family. list-declarations reports the facade directive with its alias and leaves local declaration flags unchanged.

- [PROJ-1] Loads shared module dependencies once in dependency order and releases state on allocation failure.
- [PROJ-2] Reports module cycles with both the importing location and the related source location.
- [PROJ-MODULE-NAME-FIX-1] `E_MODULE_NAME` states the rule and the fix: it names the declared header, the file, and the module name that file must declare (its path from the project root without `.my`), and ends with the exact header to write — for `sexpr/parser.my` declaring `module parser`, the message ends `write module sexpr.parser` with the header quoted.
- [PROJ-3] Rejects duplicate exports, missing exported symbols, private types exposed through public interfaces, and a re-export whose qualifier names no import or whose target is not exported in its defining module.
- [PROJ-4] Reports related declarations in their original source files after project source mapping.
- [PROJ-5] `mylang run FILE` loads the reachable project graph when the entry file opens with a `module` header or a module-only `import`/`export` directive, so a single file that imports a standard module needs no separate command; a file with no module header stays one standalone unit, and `run-project` remains the explicit project-entry alias.

- [PROJ-6] Project loading defaults to 4,096 reachable modules and 65,536 import edges, up from 256 and 4,096; a `mylang.json` `budgets` object overrides `max_modules`, `max_imports`, `max_file_bytes` and `max_total_bytes`. Each present value must be a positive integer, unknown and duplicate budget keys are rejected like every other configuration field, and an absent field keeps its default.
- [PROJ-7] `mylang build FILE` detects a module-project entry the same way `run` does and links the reachable graph without requiring `--project`; a plain single-file program keeps the one-unit path, and `--project` remains an explicit override.
- [QBE-ARRAYWRITE-1] A `replace` on a fixed array writes one element of the local it read instead of copying the whole array twice: when the array operand is that local's own storage — a `load_local` or `move_local` of it that ran with an empty operand stack and is reached by falling through, so the borrow rule already named the slot — and the very next instruction stores the replaced array back into that same local, the element is written straight into the local's slot after the same unsigned bounds check against the same length, so the copy into the per-pc aggregate scratch, the copy back out of it and the now-dead scratch declaration all disappear and an element write costs one check and one store instead of two whole-array copies. The destination is claimed only when the local owns nothing, is this frame's own nonzero-sized value of the array's own type rather than a parameter slot holding a caller's address, and nothing between the read and the replace is entered by a branch or writes the local — so a copy taken before the write, an array inside a record, an array a callee was handed and a table read through another live operand all still see the value they held, and an out-of-range index still traps `R_LIST_BOUNDS` with the same index, length and source position.
- [QBE-ARRAYWRITE-2] An element write through a record field, `r.cells[i] = v` on a value record local and `c.cells[i] = v` on a `class` reference (and their augmented forms), writes the one element where the field already lives instead of copying the array and the record: when the statement is exactly the checker's `load r; load r; get_field k; <index>; <value>; replace_index; update_record {k}; store r` over one local, entered by no branch, with nothing between the field read and the `replace` but scalar pushes and computations that cannot call, allocate or write the local, the field is a fixed array of scalar elements, and a value record is this frame's own storage owning nothing, the field read names the field inside the record (the frame slot or the heap cell), the `replace` stores the element there after the same unsigned bounds check with the same trap and position, and the update and the store copy nothing. Any other shape — a table or an array of aggregates, a record reached through a parameter slot holding a caller's address, a write below a list slot, a value computed by a call — keeps the rebuilding lowering and its copies.
- [QBE-FIELDARRAY-READ-1] Reading one element of a `class` field that is a fixed array of scalars, `c.cells[i]`, loads the element from the heap cell instead of first snapshotting the whole array: the field read lends the field's address when its only consumer is the element read that follows it and every instruction between them is a scalar push or computation that cannot call, allocate or write memory, so the cell still holds what the snapshot would have copied. A field read with any other consumer, or across a call, keeps its snapshot.
- [QBE-ARRAYREF-1] A `mut` fixed-array argument crosses the call as the address of the caller's own storage: the front end emits `ref_local` for it instead of a load, which pushes the local's slot address (or, for a `mut` array parameter passed on, the caller's address that slot already holds). The callee's `mut` array parameter is an address-holding slot that is never copied at entry; its element writes go through that address, forwarded in place by `QBE-ARRAYWRITE-1` when the write is a statement, and any other store to it copies into the caller's storage. A read of it that the borrow rule cannot name — because the window before its consumer writes the parameter or hands it to a `ref_local` — is a snapshot in the per-pc scratch, and so is a read of any local whose `ref_local` is already on the operand stack, so `f(mut a, a)` hands the read-only parameter a copy. `ref_local` counts as a write of the local wherever the backend proves a local unwritten (read-only parameter addressing, payload projection, element and call-result borrows, constructor and replace forwarding), the bytecode validator accepts it only on a fixed-array local, and the inliner never splices a callee with a `mut` array parameter. Roots are unchanged: the caller's slot stays a traced root of its own frame while the callee writes managed values into it, and a callee's address-holding parameter is traced through the address.
- [QBE-AGGREF-9] A parameter that crosses by address (`QBE-AGGREF-1`) and a match payload binding that names its payload in place (`QBE-MATCHPROJ-1`) each occupy one word of the frame — the slot that holds the address — rather than the value's whole size, in the slot's declaration and in the frame estimate alike, and a caller charges one word of outgoing argument space for every argument that crosses as a scalar or as an address, the value's own size only for a move-only aggregate that still crosses by value. The slot holds the same address, is read through it exactly as before and is never zero-filled, copied into or released, so every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-CTORREAD-1] A record or variant constructor ends the life of each field operand it reads, so a local whose value one of those operands is — and whose type releases nothing — is named by its own slot instead of snapshotted into per-pc aggregate scratch, exactly as `QBE-AGGREF-1`'s borrow rule already did for a field, tag, element or argument read. The constructor writes each field once, into this pc's own scratch or into a destination no reachable instruction reads, so the region a named operand points at is never the region being written; a local the window writes, a branch entering the window, and an owning or zero-sized type all keep the copy, and every value, trap and trace is unchanged.
- [QBE-RETFWD-1] A list or byte-string element read whose only consumer is the `return` that hands it back is returned from the receiver's own backing storage: the result leaves through the one copy the caller owns — `%out` for a result over 4 KiB, the frame's return storage for a heap-backed frame, QBE's return pad otherwise — so the copy into per-pc aggregate scratch and the reload out of it disappear and the element crosses the boundary once. Only the epilogue runs in between, so the address is kept only where the epilogue can neither release nor free what it names: a frame that drops a local or closes an arena scope keeps the snapshot, as does an element the caller would have to release, one of zero size, a read a branch can enter directly, and a result type that is not the element's own. Bounds checking, negative-index normalization, the trap's code, message, position and structured detail, and the value the caller receives are unchanged.
- [QBE-CALLREF-1] A local whose only write is the store of a record-returning call's result — `x = f(...)`, and `x = f(...).field` through an uninterrupted chain of field reads — holds the address of that result instead of a copy of it, so the whole-record copy into the local and the per-pc scratch it came from both disappear and every read of `x` reaches the result where the callee left it.
