The storage is the frame's own: one result buffer per call site, reserved in the entry block, which lives as long as the frame and is rewritten only by that same call site — which re-runs the store first — so no live-range proof is needed and no instruction between the store and the last read is restricted. The local's slot becomes one word; a local that traces starts out naming an all-zero value of its own type, so a root push before the store still finds a traceable record. A local that is written anywhere else, is a parameter, owns something, is zero-sized, or whose type is not the callee's result type keeps its copy, and every value, trap, trace, arena check and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-2] `x = xs[i]` names its element where it lives on the same terms `QBE-ELEMENT-ADDRESS-1` gave `for x in xs`, and the run that must be inert is now the borrow's real live range rather than the branch-free window after the store: every instruction reachable from the store without the store running again, from which a read of the local is reachable, together with the run after each read over which the operand that read pushed is still on the stack — which is where a `return x` and a call handed `x` sit. Inside that range only instructions that can neither call, allocate, release nor mutate are admitted; control flow, another element read of a list, byte string or fixed array, and a length read join the set, while any call, append, element write or unknown opcode ends the claim. A `return x` inside the range additionally needs an epilogue that releases nothing. An element type that holds managed references is borrowed too, and its slot is never read by the root protocol: no collection can run inside an inert range, and outside it the local is dead, so a root push can never follow an address into storage the receiver has since reallocated or the sweep has freed — while everything the element names stays reachable through the receiver itself. A frame that opens an arena scope keeps the copy, because closing the scope scans every local declared before it through its slot. A local written more than once is claimed only when every write stores an address of the same type and the same stability — a compiler temporary the front end reuses for two element reads of the same receiver is exactly that shape — and each write then carries its own live-range proof over a range the other writes end, so the slot holds an address for the whole function and never an address and then a value. Value-snapshot semantics are unchanged: a copy taken before a write to the same element still holds what it held, because the write is not inert and ends the claim.
- [QBE-LISTSET-1] Writing one element of a managed list or byte string — `xs[i] = v` and `replace(xs, i, v)` on a list receiver — is emitted as the same unsigned bounds test against the same receiver length followed by one store of the element's own size through `storage + i * stride`, in place of the `myl_native_list_set` call that performed exactly those two steps. An out-of-range or unrepresentable index still traps `R_LIST_BOUNDS` with the same index, length, message and source position, negative-index normalization is unchanged, the arena store barrier still runs before the bounds test so an escaping value still reports `E_ARENA_ESCAPE` first, and the list's length, capacity, generation and identity are untouched, so no iteration guard can observe the write. Because the write now names no allocating callee, it is no longer a safepoint: the root record this frame used to push for every live traced local and operand across the call, and its restore, are not emitted, and a value that names storage inside the same receiver copies one stride-aligned region onto an identical or disjoint one, exactly as the runtime helper's `memcpy` did.
- [QBE-FRAMEFIT-1] Whether a function's frame lives on the native stack or in a per-call heap block is decided from the per-pc aggregate scratch its lowered body actually declares, not from every scratch buffer the lowering might have declared: a function whose conservative estimate exceeds the 32 KiB native limit is lowered onto the native stack first, its frame is measured again from the `%agg`/`%aux` declarations that survived constructor forwarding, the borrow rule, the in-place array write and dead-slot removal, and only a frame that still exceeds the limit is lowered a second time, heap-backed, at the conservative size. A callee that only reads a large fixed-array parameter — `kernel(values: [1024]int)` returning `values[0]`, or the same over `[4096]int` — therefore calls neither `myl_qbe_frame_allocate` nor `myl_qbe_frame_release`, and neither does a function that only passes such a parameter on; a function whose surviving scratch or own locals exceed the limit still gets a heap block large enough for every slot its prologue derives from it, the stack-floor guard stays on every non-leaf and on every leaf whose frame could reach the headroom, a lowering that fails for any reason fails the same way once, and every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged.
- [QBE-NORETURN-1] In a build that dropped both step and frame accounting — the fast protocol of `QBE-UNWIND-1`, which a ReleaseFast executable and `mylang run` use when no ceiling was asked for — the cold path of every check whose last call is a `myl_qbe_fail_*` helper — or the `myl_qbe_int_neg`/`myl_qbe_int_binary` call in the cold block a checked negation or shift opens for the operands that always trap — ends in `hlt` (LLVM's `unreachable`) instead of `jmp @failure` into the shared exit that unlinks the frame and returns. The trampoline armed the non-local exit before any guest instruction ran, and every such helper records the trap, builds the trace and leaves through it, so the jump was dead; without it a check is no longer an edge into one common exit, which had kept checked functions from inlining and hidden their no-overflow facts. Every check is still performed, and a trap's code, message, `in f at file:line` trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record and exit status are byte-identical. A build with step or frame accounting — a library, the guest-test runner, a Debug executable, a run with `--max-steps` or `--max-frames` — keeps the sticky return path unchanged. The LLVM adapter accepts `hlt`, declares every `myl_qbe_fail_*` helper `cold`, and declares one `noreturn` only in a unit where every call to it is followed by `hlt`.
- [QBE-FRAMEMAP-1] In the fast protocol with the value guards on — a ReleaseFast executable or `mylang run` with no `--max-steps`/`--max-frames` ceiling — a guest function links no frame record: no `ctx->active_frame` load or store, no metadata store and no position store runs on any call path. Each unit opens with `dbgfile "SYMBOL"`, every call is preceded by `dbgloc PC+1` naming the bytecode instruction it belongs to (pc 0 for the prologue's own calls), and the prologue keeps only the execution-stack floor test, whose `myl_qbe_fail_frame` call carries column 1 to mark it as an entry guard. The build turns those positions into a table of return addresses, and when a trap fires the runtime walks the guest stack — by call-frame information in an LLVM program, whose guest functions carry unwind tables and no frame pointer and which links libunwind, and by the frame-pointer chain in a QBE program — and reports every return address found in the table — skipping an entry guard's frame, which was never entered — expanding bytecode-inliner splices exactly as before. A trap's code, message, `in f at file:line` trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record, `assert` message and exit status are byte-identical to the linked-record protocol, including a callee LLVM inlined, a trap inside an inlined runtime helper, recursion and a stack-floor failure. A build with step or frame accounting, a library, the guest-test runner, a Debug executable and an unchecked build are unchanged. For the QBE walk, the generated C and every runtime unit that can reach `fail` (the `qbe_*` units and the text methods) keep a frame pointer in every function that calls; the heap, collector and container units, which only return a status, keep every register.
- [LLVM-OVERFLOW-INTRINSIC-1] The LLVM adapter lowers a checked 64-bit `+`, `-` or `*` to `llvm.{s,u}{add,sub,mul}.with.overflow.i64`, so it reaches the machine as the operation and one flag branch (`add; jo`, `sub; jb`, `imul; jo`) instead of the frontend's xor/and sign test or four-limb high product. It evaluates each block symbolically — a pure 64-bit operation is its opcode over its operands' values, a literal is its bit pattern, anything else is a value equal only to itself — and rewrites a flag only when its value is exactly one of the frontend's formulas over an `add`, `sub` or `mul` earlier in the same block: `((a ^ r) & (b ^ r)) < 0` for a signed add, `((a ^ b) & (a ^ r)) < 0` for a signed subtract, `r <u a` and `a <u b` for the unsigned ones, the four-limb high product `!= 0` for an unsigned multiply, and the signed high product against `r >> 63` for a signed one. Each formula is the intrinsic's overflow bit for every pair of 64-bit inputs, so the rewrite assumes nothing about the operands; the intrinsic's value replaces the wrapped result bit for bit. The branch, the cold block and its `myl_qbe_fail_overflow_op` call with the same operands, width and position are untouched, so a trap's code, message, trace, `MYLANG-TRAP` record and exit status are byte-identical. A narrower width's range test and the one-compare test against a literal operand are left as they are, the QBE backend's IL and assembly do not change, and an `--unchecked` build, which emits no flag, and `wrapping_*`/`saturating_*` are unaffected.
- [QBE-LOOP-FACTS-1] With its value guards on and no step accounting, the native emitter plans each innermost loop of the shape `for i in range(...)`, `for x in xs`, `enumerate(...)` and `while i < n` / `while i <= n` with one `i = i + step` as the loop's last statement (a positive literal step, or a step held in a local the loop never writes), whose every instruction is a local load or store, a literal, a comparison, a jump, integer or float arithmetic, a cast, a field or fixed-array operation, `float`/`int`/`sqrt`-style float conversions, or a list or `bytes` length, element read, element write or iteration check — no call, no allocation, no pop, insert, slice or other resizing operation, no `str` or dictionary access, and at most one append — to a list of integers, `bool` or floats held in a local, in a straight-line body with a literal step, which is then the loop's only access to lists of that type — and whose header is its only entry. Such a loop cannot change the length of any collection it reads or move any element storage. The plan names the value of every operand and local symbolically (literals, locals the loop never writes, the counter between its entry value and its bound, an accumulator whose one write adds a bounded amount, sums, differences and products of those, lengths, a division of a bounded value by a nonzero literal, and elements read at an invariant position from a list whose element slots the loop never writes) and proves: an index `alpha * i + beta` with `alpha` in -1, 0, 1 and an invariant `beta` is in bounds on every iteration when its smallest value is at least zero and its largest is below the length, which also makes the frontend's `< 0` normalization branch for that index dead; a checked 64-bit `+`, `-` or `*` whose operands are bounded cannot overflow once every leaf it is computed from lies in `[-2^b, 2^b]`, where `b` (at most 60, at least 20) is the widest bound at which every such expression, counted as coefficient times leaf-bound to its degree, fits 64 bits — an operation that fits only below 2^20 keeps its check; an accumulator is bounded by its entry value plus three times the leaf bound times its increment's bound; a `for x in xs` length check holds on every iteration when it holds on entry; an invariant row `c[i]` and a stable list's element storage address are the same on every iteration; and an append runs once per counter value, so the list's spare capacity holds every iteration's element when it is at least the number of counter values. A loop the plan proves nothing about, and every loop of a step-accounted or `--unchecked` build, is lowered once, exactly as before.
- [QBE-LOOP-VERSION-1] A planned loop (`QBE-LOOP-FACTS-1`) is lowered twice behind a guard that every entry to the loop reaches first: it checks that the counter has at least 16 values to run through before its bound (a shorter loop saves fewer checks than the guard costs, and runs the original), each leaf's magnitude bound, a non-negative (or, for an accumulator, positive) step held in a local, each proven index's extremes against its length, each iteration check's length against its expected value, and reads each hoisted row and element storage address once, in an order in which no element is read before its position was checked. When every condition holds, control runs a check-free copy of the loop: the proven index, overflow and iteration checks and the dead normalization branches are omitted, an append whose room the guard checked (`capacity - length` at least the number of counter values) is an element store with the length and generation written back exactly as the inline append's fast path writes them, without its capacity test or growth call,, a hoisted row is the guard's value, and an element address is computed from the guard's storage address; every check the plan did not prove is still emitted, at the same instruction, with the same trap, operands and position. When any condition fails, control runs the original loop, lowered exactly as an unversioned build lowers it, so a trap the guard could not rule out is raised by the original at the same iteration with the same code, message, trace and `MYLANG-TRAP` record. The copy is discarded if its lowering would name an allocating callee. Results, traps and trace text are byte-identical to the unversioned lowering; only the instructions executed differ.
- [LLVM-ELEMENT-CLASS-1] Emitted QBE IL marks the storage word of a managed container — the start of its element storage block — with `# myl-elem %temp KIND` instead of `# myl-obj %temp`, where KIND is the element's byte size followed by `r` when elements are GC references and `v` otherwise: the storage word an element read or write loads, the backing pointer of the inline list append, and the destination and source storage of the inline byte-buffer appends. It is a comment line, so QBE's assembly is byte-identical. The LLVM adapter reads it as everything `# myl-obj` says and more: an access derived by `copy` and offsetting `add` from an element storage word is placed in an element scope of its kind, one derived from any other marked object (a header, an allocation's payload) in an object scope, and the scoped alias metadata says objects and element storage never alias, and neither do element blocks of different kinds — each storage block is an allocation of its own and only ever holds elements of its one kind. A plain foreign access (a parameter, a global, the context) still aliases all of them, an access that combines two refined classes drops the refinement for the whole unit, and a unit with more than 29 element kinds leaves the rest plainly foreign. It is what lets LLVM keep a list's length, storage pointer and row pointers in registers across a loop that stores into element storage.
- [LLVM-ELEMENT-GEP-1] The LLVM adapter gives a load or store whose address is an element address — a singly-defined `add` computed earlier in the same block from a marked storage word or object plus at least one non-constant term, every temporary on the way singly defined and none redefined in between — the address as a plain (not `inbounds`) `getelementptr i8` from the marked base instead of an `inttoptr` of the integer sum. The bits are the same; LLVM's scalar evolution can now see the variable part, so a loop over `data[i]` loads whole vectors instead of gathering one element at a time, and dependence distances can be computed. The integer `add` is still emitted for every other reader, a constant-offset header field and every `blit` keep the integer form, and `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_ELEMENT_GEP=0` turns it off.
- [LLVM-STICKY-REDUCTION-1] Before translation, the LLVM adapter versions an innermost loop whose overflow checks do not decide control or an address: the loop's entry edges go to a fast copy in which the check of a reduction — a 64-bit signed frame cell the loop changes only by storing `v + x`, `x + v` or `v - x` where `v` is a value the cell held — is dropped while the copy sums the magnitudes of the added values, and the check of any other value is sticky (its flag is OR-ed into one word and the copy continues with the wrapped result). Every other check stays a branch, to a fallback. At each normal exit the copy verifies that the sticky word is zero and that `|entry value| + sum |x| <= 2^63 - 1` for every reduction (with the per-site iteration limits that keep the sums exact); then its results are the checked loop's. Otherwise, and on every would-be trap, the fallback restores every frame cell the loop writes to its entry value and runs the original loop, whose checks trap exactly as before: same code, message, operands, trace, record and exit status. The rewrite applies only to loops with no call, `blit` or phi, whose stores all write this activation's own `alloc` cells, whose temporaries are not live across the loop boundary, and in which no value that may have wrapped reaches a non-check branch, a load or store address, a shift amount, a divisor or a float-to-integer conversion; a check whose wrapped value would is kept as a branch. The QBE backend is unaffected, and `MYLANG_QBE_LLVM_STICKY=0` turns it off.
- [QBE-FRAMEMAP-ASM-1] A frame-map unit's assembly is rewritten before it is assembled, and a full-LTO frame-map executable is optimized by LLD to one assembly file that is rewritten, assembled and linked natively: every call a `.loc` line (QBE, or LLVM without inlining) or a `.pseudoprobe` directive (LLVM) positions is followed by a local label, and a retained `mylang_frame_map` section records, per call site, one entry per logical frame innermost first — a probe's inline stack adds each guest caller the call was inlined through at its own call-site position — each naming the unit's metadata and the position, with bits marking an entry guard, an inlined caller, and an entry from assembly that carries call-frame information (LLVM's), whose program the runtime unwinds by that information. The `.file`, `.loc` and `.pseudoprobe` directives and the probe descriptors are removed, so no DWARF reaches the object. Assembly with no positions is left byte-identical, a position that names an unknown unit or a call positioned in another unit's function without an inlining record fails the build, and the compiler and the native build share the guard column.
- [QBE-ROOTLIVE-1] A safepoint publishes a root record only for the traced locals that are live at it — read by the safepoint itself or by an instruction reachable from it before the local is written again — instead of for every traced local the frame declares. Liveness is a backward dataflow over the bytecode's own successor relation (branches, `?` error edges and match arms, where an arm's payload binding is written on the edge into it), so a hidden receiver temporary the front end made for one `xs[...]` expression stops being a root after its last read, a named local stops being one after its last read, and a value read at a loop's head stays one across the whole body. Operand-stack values are published exactly as before. The lowering's implicit reads count as reads: closing an arena scope scans every local declared before it, the fused `src[a:b].decode()` hands the slice's receiver to the decode two instructions later, and a local whose type owns something is released through its slot by the epilogue and by an overwriting store, so it stays published at every safepoint; a function with an arena scope and no root push keeps its locals' zero-fill for that scan. Collection only ever frees what no later instruction reads, so every value, trap, trace and exit status is unchanged, under `--gc-stress` too.
- [QBE-INVOKE-SAFEPOINT-1] Invoking a function value is a safepoint: the callee is whichever top-level function the value names, known only at run time, so the invoking instruction publishes its live roots around the indirect call and a function that invokes one counts as able to allocate, exactly like a direct call to a function that can. A list the caller still reads after invoking a callback that allocates therefore survives the collection that allocation starts.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-3] A cast to an integer type is inert inside a borrow's live range (`QBE-ELEMREF-2`): it only range-checks and traps, like the checked arithmetic already admitted, and it is how a runtime index's negative-index normalization converts the receiver length — so a second element read with a runtime index, such as `pins[row.index]` while `row = rows[i]` is live, no longer cancels the borrow. A cast to text builds a string and still ends the claim.
- [QBE-ELEMREF-4] A direct call inside a borrow's live range (`QBE-ELEMREF-2`) keeps the borrow when the callee provably cannot disturb the receiver's backing storage: the allocation analysis proved it cannot allocate — directly, transitively or through a function value — so it can neither collect nor grow a list; none of its parameter types traces a reference, so, with no mutable globals, no closures and no allocation, it can name no heap object at all; and it has no mutation footprint and writes no aggregate parameter, so no `mut` parameter reaches storage the caller names. `for k, s in enumerate(spheres): t = intersect(origin, direction, s)` with a plain-data `Sphere` therefore reads each sphere in place instead of copying it per iteration. A callee that can allocate, takes a list, class, string or other traced parameter, or declares or infers a `mut` parameter keeps the copy, and every value, trap and trace is unchanged.
- [QBE-NICHE-1] An `Option` whose `Some` payload is a managed reference — a class instance, list, dict, `str`, `bytes` or `Cell`, every type whose value is a non-null heap pointer — is stored as that reference word alone: 8 bytes, 8-aligned, null for `None`, and the case is whether the word is null. Every other optional keeps the `{tag, payload}` layout: a scalar, record, union or domain payload, and an optional optional, whose inner `None` already owns the null word, so `Some(None)` and `None` stay distinct. The rule reads the schema's `some`/`none` cases, so a user union of exactly that shape is laid out the same way. A `class` with two optional children is 16 bytes of payload rather than 32. The runtime needs no kind of its own for it: the optional's descriptor is its payload's pointer descriptor under the optional's name, because the collector, the arena region check and escape copier, and structural equality already treat a null pointer as no reference, so they trace nothing for `None`, copy it as null and compare it equal only to `None`. The native layout ABI version is `mylang-native-amd64-layout-v3`.
- [QBE-NICHE-2] Nothing a program can observe changes with `QBE-NICHE-1`: construction, `is None`/`is not None` and the narrowing they give, `match` with and without a binding, `?` on an optional, `is_some`/`is_none`/`unwrap_or`, `==`/`!=`, printing and f-string rendering (`Some(...)`/`None`), elements of lists, dict values, record fields, fixed-array elements and class fields, arguments and results of direct calls and callbacks, and `escape` out of an arena all answer exactly what the tagged layout answered, and the collector keeps every `Some` reference alive and follows no `None`, including under `--gc-stress`.
- [QBE-SIZE-LIMIT-1] Before QBE compiles a guest function, the build estimates QBE's memory for it as 1.6 bytes per block x temporary pair (its liveness and register allocation keep one temporary bit set per block) and, when the estimate exceeds three quarters of the address-space ceiling QBE inherits from the compiler, refuses the build with a message naming the function, its blocks and temporaries, the estimate, the ceiling and the two ways out (split the function or build with `--backend llvm`), exit status 2, instead of letting QBE die in `emalloc` after minutes. An unbounded ceiling refuses nothing, and a function that fits builds exactly as before.
- [QBE-NICHE-3] The copied-value boundary keeps its external form for a niche optional: the codec manifest describes it with its own kind, `niche`, and the adapter reads its case from whether the reference word is null and writes `None` as the null word, so the wire node still carries the case index in `bits` and the payload as its one child, exactly as for a tagged optional. The host bridge never carries a traced value, so its schema is unchanged.

## Discovery and formatting

- [TOOL-1] Groups exact duplicate bodies after binding renaming while preserving resolved binding identity.
- [TOOL-2] Reports literal differences once per exact-group representative when discovering near duplicates.
- [TOOL-3] Reports incomplete near-duplicate discovery when work budgets are exhausted and rejects invalid budgets.
- [TOOL-5] `fmt` prints the one canonical layout to stdout, permits semantically incomplete drafts but rejects syntactically invalid input, and `--check` is nonzero when the input bytes differ from canonical output. The fixed layout uses four-space suites; one space around binary/assignment operators and after commas and annotation colons; no padding inside ordinary delimiters; no trailing whitespace; one final newline for nonempty source; two blank lines between top-level declarations and one after the header directive group. Comment attachment, literal values, directive order and expression evaluation order are preserved, and there is no configurable wrapping.
- [FMT-MODULE-DETECT-1] `fmt` formats a module file instead of rejecting it: a source whose first line that is neither blank nor a comment opens with a `module` header or an `import` directive is formatted exactly as `fmt-module` formats it, so `fmt FILE --check` works on every file the compiler accepts.
- [FMT-CLAUSE-SPACE-1] `fmt` and `fmt-module` keep one space between an identifier and a `]` that closes a type application or subscript, so a signature whose return type ends in brackets keeps its clause a separate word (`-> Option[int] effects():`, `-> list[T] noalloc:`, including inside a callback type) instead of printing `Option[int]effects()`; the `]` of an array length prefix still binds an identifier element type (`[2]u.Value`), and the output is idempotent.
- [TOOL-6] Includes calls in untaken paths and cycles when calculating static function reachability.
## Evaluation and wire boundaries

- [WIRE-2] Rejects duplicate input names, keywords, unsupported input types, and noncanonical or out-of-range values.
- [WIRE-3] Encodes independently constructed typed values without consuming them and enforces byte, value, and depth limits.
- [WIRE-4] Rejects duplicate, missing, unknown, and private function entries in domain codec declarations.
## Command line

- [DECLARATION-LIST-1] `list-declarations` prints one parse-only `declarations-v1` JSON document with the declared module name or null for a single-file program, its import names and explicit aliases, its export directives (a re-export carries its qualifier as `alias`), and every top-level declaration's kind, name, export flag, byte range and attached comment start. The kinds are exactly the top-level constructs the parser accepts: alias, const, struct, enum, union, result, domain, resource, fn and host_fn. An export directive sets the named local declaration's flag, a re-export marks no local declaration, and unknown and duplicate export directives reject with the existing located diagnostics. Listing requires only parsing, so modules with type or ownership errors still list, and no name resolution, checking or execution is claimed.
- [INTERFACE-SIGNATURE-1] `list-interfaces` prints each exported callable's whole signature by default: parameters in their Python type spelling (capabilities as `Filesystem`, `Network`, `Clock`, `Entropy`, `Environment`, never the removed `*_cap` internal names), the inferred `effects(...)` set when the body checks, `mutates(...)` by parameter name, and the declared ceiling when the source wrote one. A body that fails to check leaves the declaration listed and reports no body diagnostic, so the interface gate stays declaration-only. `--contracts` renders the same Python capability spellings in its canonical signature text.
- [INTERFACE-SIGNATURE-CLI-1] The `list-interfaces` command renders a capability entry parameter as its Python spelling (`Filesystem`, `Clock`) rather than the removed internal tag, including for a single-file program that exports `main`.
- [BUILD-TIMINGS-1] A native build's `--timings=json` request is carried to the linker step as an argument rather than as a new build-option field, the usage text names it, and only that spelling is a build control: a bare `--timings`, a value attached to it, and the switch written after `--` (where it belongs to the guest program) are rejected or ignored exactly as an ordinary build control is.

- [DECLARATION-LIST-2] Listed byte ranges are zero-based, half-open, disjoint and ordered by start. A declaration runs from its first token through its terminating semicolon or final token before an implicit newline/EOF terminator, and its comment start covers contiguous leading `#` lines with no blank line before the declaration. The module header, import statements, export directives and comment-and-declaration ranges account for every byte of the file except whitespace and line comments between them.

- [DEPENDENCIES-1] `project-dependencies` emits a versioned checked module graph with resolved imports, source byte hashes, implementation identity and exact consumed generated-input manifest hashes. It does not execute guest code; invalid projects emit no complete receipt.

- [DISK-PROJECT-1] Interpreter application hosts load `main.my` and imported modules from an explicit disk directory, with bounded reads and no embedded-source fallback. Failed loads and compilation release owned storage and preserve located source errors; a later invocation reads repaired source. Existing typed host registrations remain mandatory. The standalone acceptance driver also changes guest source and project roots using the same executable without Zig or Guardian on PATH.

- [CLI-1] Distinguishes source errors, runtime traps, and invalid command usage through actual process results.
- [ARGV-1] A guest entry point may take `args: list[str]` as its first parameter; `mylang run` and `build --run` reserve their own options before `--` and pass every value after it as guest arguments, installed executables pass OS arguments after `argv[0]`, and generated native entry points build the same managed list with validated UTF-8 text.
- [NATIVE-ARGV-1] A native executable from `mylang build` hands `main(args)` every argument after `argv[0]` unchanged — `--`, `--gc-stress`, `--print-result`, `--max-steps=N`, `--restrict` and `--allow-*` included — and reads its runtime options only from the `MYLANG_OPTIONS` environment variable: the `--restrict`, `--allow-*`, `--max-steps=N`, `--max-frames=N`, `--timeout=SECONDS`, `--gc-stress` and `--print-result` spellings `mylang run` takes, separated by whitespace, where a double-quoted stretch keeps its whitespace and a backslash inside it escapes the next character. An unknown, malformed or repeated option, or an unterminated quote, exits 2 before the first guest instruction. `mylang run`, `run-project` and `build --run` keep their own command lines: the runtime options given before `--` travel to the program in `MYLANG_OPTIONS`, replacing any inherited value, and only the values after `--` reach its argv.
- [CHECK-PROJECT-ENTRY-1] `mylang check ENTRY` where ENTRY opens as a module project checks the reachable project graph exactly like `check-project`, executing nothing; a plain single-file program keeps the one-unit path unchanged.
- [EPRINT-1] `eprint` is the standard-error spelling of `print`: it accepts the same values and `sep=`/`end=` keywords, writes only stderr, contributes the same `output` effect, and shares the buffered one-line native output path without mixing bytes between streams.
- [STREAM-CAPABILITY-1] `Stream` is a capability passed like `Filesystem`. The entry point may declare `out: Stream` and `err: Stream`; the entry rule matches Stream parameters by name, admits at most one of each, and rejects any other name with `E_MAIN`. `stream.print(values..., sep=, end=)` renders like the old printing builtin and `stream.write(text) -> int` writes the text and returns its character count; both contribute the `output` effect and take the destination from the receiver value, so a helper declared `def show(log: Stream)` may be handed either `out` or `err`. Streams are granted unconditionally, including under `--restrict`, and the per-type native renderers write through the stream's own sentinel. A bare `print`/`eprint` reports `E_OUTPUT_CAPABILITY` with the fix to add `out: Stream` and pass one from main; a local binding or declared function named `print` still shadows the spelling.
- [STREAM-BYTES-1] `stream.write(data: bytes) -> int` writes the raw octets, including NUL and invalid UTF-8, without quoting or an added newline, flushes the selected stream, and returns the byte count. The existing `str` overload still returns its Unicode scalar count. Both preserve destination capability and the `output` effect, reject other argument types, and trap `R_OUTPUT` on a refused write or flush; byte output performs no intermediate text conversion or managed allocation.
- [STREAM-BYTES-2] `stream.write(buffer)` accepts the mutable `byte_buffer` and writes the octets it holds at that moment straight out of the buffer's own storage: no snapshot is built, no copy is made, and the write contributes the same `output` effect and returns the same byte count as writing a byte string, so an `effects(output)` function may call it and `noio` withholds it with `E_EFFECT_BOUND`. Appending to the buffer afterwards cannot change what was already written, the lowered form is one raw byte-sink call naming the receiver, and a program that only writes a buffer links no copy, slice or snapshot helper.
- [DEBUG-LINE-1] `debug(values...)` renders like `print` to standard error, takes no capability, contributes no effect, returns unit, and is emitted only by Debug builds and by `run`/`test`; a debug line therefore never changes a signature or a checked effect bound.
- [RESULT-MAIN-1] A guest `main` may return `Result[int, str]`: `Ok(code)` becomes the process status `code & 0xFF`, while `Err(message)` writes exactly one `message` line to stderr and exits 1; runtime traps and source/usage failures keep their existing distinct statuses. The synthesized entry wrapper that renders the error is the runtime's exit protocol and is not held to `main`'s signature-derived effect ceiling, so a `main` with no `out`/`err` stream parameter may still return `Err`.
- [OPTION-TRY-1] `?` propagates `None` only within a function returning a compatible `Option[T]`; a `Some(value)` unwraps to `value`, and mixing `Option` propagation with a result-returning function or vice versa is a located type error.
- [OPTION-TRY-3] Option propagation does not require the operand's element to match the enclosing function's: `?` on an `Option[T]` inside a function returning `Option[U]` unwraps `Some(value)` to a `T` and returns a fresh `None` of `Option[U]`, for any `T` and `U` (scalar, managed or class, concrete or generic), because `None` carries no payload. Propagating an option into a function that does not return an `Option` still rejects with `E_TRY_RETURN`.
- [BOUNDARY-CLI-1] `examples/boundary-cli` is a two-module acceptance program that combines `args: list[str]`, `Filesystem`, explicit `Environment`, `print`, `eprint`, source-file reads, and `main -> Result[int, str]`, and the same cases pass through `run`, `build --run`, and an installed executable.
- [CAPABILITY-METHOD-NARROWING-1] `fs.read_only() -> Filesystem` narrows a `Filesystem` capability to read authority by intersecting its permission mask. The derived capability is an ordinary typed value: it can be passed, returned, stored in records/classes, and cannot be stored in a list or `Cell`. It performs no I/O and contributes no effect.
- [CAPABILITY-METHOD-NARROWING-2] `restrict_resource[...]` is removed and reports `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` naming `fs.read_only()`; `acquire_resource`, `complete_resource`, `read_resource` and `write_resource` are unchanged; `environment_get[...]` is now a trusted standard-library spelling only.
- [FILESYSTEM-DIR-1] `fs.dir(path) -> Filesystem` derives a filesystem rooted at the string-literal `path`, resolved under the receiver's root. It records the scope lazily in a bounded host-side table, performs no I/O, and contributes no effect. Every acquisition through the derived capability joins the prefix and rejects a request that could leave the root (`..`, `.`, empty components, backslashes or NUL); `restrict_resource` preserves the scope. A dynamic path or a literal that escapes the root is `E_FILESYSTEM_DIR` at check time, and a non-`Filesystem` receiver is rejected.
- [FILE-METHOD-1] Every whole-path file helper also accepts a qualified method spelling on a `Filesystem` value: `fs.open(path, mode)`, `fs.read_text(path)`, `fs.write_text(path, text)`, `fs.read_file(path)`, `fs.write_file(path, data)`, `fs.exists(path)`, `fs.listdir(path)`, `fs.mkdir(path)` and `fs.remove(path)`. The receiver is the capability operand, so the method form has one fewer argument than the free spelling, and both share the same arities, result types, `E_ARITY`, the literal-mode `E_FILE_ARGUMENT` rule and the trapping `with fs.open(...) as f:` head. The method belongs to the receiver, so a declared function or local named `read_text` still claims the free `read_text(fs, path)` spelling, and generic bodies keep rejecting the file builtins under either spelling.
- [NETWORK-HOST-1] `net.host(name) -> Network` narrows a `Network` capability to the single string-literal endpoint `name`. It records the scope lazily in the same bounded host-side table as `fs.dir`, performs no I/O, and contributes no effect. Every acquisition through the derived capability requests exactly the recorded endpoint, so a narrowed `Network` composes with a `connect` wrapper that takes no request string; an endpoint outside the active `--allow-endpoint` grant is still denied. Re-narrowing to a different endpoint traps `R_CAPABILITY`. A dynamic, empty or NUL-bearing name is `E_NETWORK_HOST` at check time, and the receiver must be a local `Network`.
- [NETWORK-ENDPOINT-1] `mylang run`, `run-project` and `build --run` accept a repeatable `--allow-endpoint=IP:PORT` grant for a `Network` entry point under `--restrict`. The endpoint is a literal numeric IPv4 address with a nonzero port; a DNS name, an IPv6 literal, an empty value and a repeat are usage errors. The grant provisions the native scoped network provider, whose allowlist matches the request verbatim (no DNS resolution or redirect following) and dials it only on an allowed acquisition; `--allow-all` grants every literal endpoint. Without the grant, `--restrict` refuses the entry point before execution with `--allow-endpoint=IP:PORT` in the message, and an endpoint outside the grant fails as `Connected.err(2)` without opening a socket. A default invocation with no grant keeps the previous behavior: the network provider stays absent and network access is denied.
- [NETWORK-SURFACE-1] The `network` standard module wraps provider acquisition and I/O so a guest does not spell the raw builtins: `connect(net)` acquires a `Connection`, `read[const N](connection)` and `write[const N](connection, data)` transfer bytes, and `close(connection)` completes the handle. The wrappers return declared `result` types and contribute the `network` effect; because resources are linear, each wrapper consumes the connection it receives.
- [ENVIRONMENT-GET-1] On an `Environment` capability value, `env.get("NAME")` returns `Option[str]`: `Some(value)` decoded as UTF-8 for a value the provider exposes and `None` when the name is absent. The name must be a literal `str`, and a dynamic name is `E_ENVIRONMENT_NAME`. A name outside the active `--allow-env` allow-list is a denied authority and traps `R_CAPABILITY` rather than answering `None`. `environment_get[...]` is accepted only in embedded standard-library sources, like the `__float_sqrt` intrinsic.
- [ENVIRONMENT-ONLY-1] `env.only("HOME", "PATH") -> Environment` narrows an `Environment` to a literal tuple of names by intersecting it with the runtime `--allow-env` allow-list. It performs no I/O and contributes no effect, and the derived capability is an ordinary typed value. Only positional string literals are accepted; a dynamic, empty or NUL-bearing name is `E_ENVIRONMENT_NAME`.
- [ENVIRONMENT-NAMES-1] `env.names() -> list[str]` returns the visible environment names as a fresh sorted managed list, honouring both the `--allow-env` allow-list and any `env.only(...)` narrowing. It takes no arguments and contributes the `environment` effect.
- [MANAGED-CELL-1] `Cell[T]` is a managed one-field reference constructed as `Cell(value=...)`; `.value` reads and replacements are shared across aliases and function calls, scalar and managed copyable contents are supported under GC stress, result resources and capabilities are rejected, and Cell values have no equality or rendering spelling.
- [CELL-COLLECTION-1] `Cell[T]` is not a collection: prelude and generic collection operations such as `sorted`, indexing, membership and search reject it with located type diagnostics instead of assuming collection storage.
- [CELL-GENERIC-1] Generic bodies recognize both `Cell(value=value)` and `Cell[T](value=value)`, infer the element type where possible, and account for the constructor's `managed_allocation` effect even when the generic declaration is otherwise unused.
- [CELL-EQUALITY-1] Equality-dependent membership, search and removal operations reject list elements and nested value graphs that contain `Cell`, including `in`, `list.contains`, `list.index`, and `list.remove`.
- [VALUE-EQUALITY-1] Values of the same non-callback, non-Cell aggregate type support `==` and `!=`: plain records, fixed arrays, unions, `Result` and `Option` compare structurally, and a managed `class` compares by reference identity. Reference cycles compare by identity, so a self-referential graph never diverges. A type whose fields reach a callback, `Cell` or resource still rejects with `E_TYPE`, and equality keeps left-to-right evaluation and the existing execution limits.
- [EQUALITY-CONTEXT-1] The two operands of `==` and `!=` share one aggregate type, and an operand reached through a field access over a known value supplies that type to a contextual constructor on the other side exactly as a plain binding does, so `hold.opt == Some("x")`, `hold.opt == None` and `hold.kind == Kind.atom("x")` type-check without an intermediate local. A field access whose base type or field is not statically known supplies nothing, so `Some("x") == Some("x")` still reports `E_OPTION_INFERENCE`.
- [OPTION-TRY-2] Generic body checking propagates `Some(value)` and `None` through `?` when the enclosing function returns a compatible `Option[T]`, and rejects propagation into a result-returning function with a located diagnostic.
- [CAPABILITY-DIAG-1] When an entry point requests several ungranted capabilities under `--restrict`, `run`, `build --run`, and installed executables stop at the first failed grant and emit exactly one identical diagnostic.
- [ARGV-LIMIT-1] Guest arguments accepted after `--` use dynamically sized storage, so `run` does not impose a lower fixed argument limit than `build --run` or an installed executable.
- [INCREMENTAL-ID-1] Module, declaration, and body identities are derived from canonical names and declaration/body content rather than source offsets; private body and comment edits preserve declaration identities and the project exported-interface fingerprint, while exported signature changes alter the interface fingerprint. The checked `project-dependencies-v1` receipt exposes these identities and fingerprints under `incremental`.
- [INCREMENTAL-INTERFACE-1] The project interface fingerprint is composed from the sorted semantic interface fingerprints of exported interface items only, so exports follow exported constants, declared effect ceilings and parameter defaults while comments, formatting, source paths, spans and private declarations are excluded.
- [EFFECT-CEILING-1] An explicit `noalloc`, `noio` or `effects(...)` on any function, exported generic template, public domain operation or exported test narrows the ceiling the signature itself implies, and is enforced at the declaration through direct, recursive, generic and callback calls; exceeding it rejects with a located witness path. The declared mask, the `mut` parameter positions and the exported return-origin bound remain part of the public contract and interface fingerprint: a body edit that stays within the ceiling preserves the interface fingerprint while the body hash and runtime-test receipts still change, a declared `mut` position change alters the function and project interface hashes, and an omitted clause leaves the ceiling the parameters imply ([DERIVED-EFFECTS-1]). Parameter defaults, keyword parameter names, nominal type identities and generic requirements remain part of the public contract.

- [EFFECT-CEILING-INFERRED-1] Exported declarations do not require an explicit effect ceiling: the published ceiling is the set the parameter list implies, narrowed by a clause when the source wrote one, so a plain `public def` is accepted and its contract is still a real bound. Pure interface pre-validation without checked bodies publishes that same derived ceiling instead of the unbounded `all` mask an omitted clause used to mean, and the published `effects` fact stays the checked body fact ([DERIVED-EFFECTS-4]).

- [EFFECT-LITERAL-1] A compile-time `str` literal contributes no `managed_allocation` in the typed effect walk or the bytecode validator, so `print("hi")` under `effects(output)` and `len("abc")` under `noalloc` are accepted; concatenation, slicing, conversion and `list`/`dict`/`bytes` literals still allocate.

- [MUTATION-OVERLAP-1] The writable/read-only overlap obligation fires only when the callee declares a write footprint — a `mut` parameter (formerly `mutates(...)`). A helper that only reads its arguments carries no obligation, so passing one shared collection to two of its parameters is accepted like Python, while a declared footprint called with possibly-aliasing read-only arguments still rejects `E_MUTATION_OVERLAP`.
- [MUTATION-METADATA-1] `effects(mutation)` names externally observable managed-state writes, and a signature may carry the optional `mutates(a, b)` clause after its effect ceiling; names normalize to sorted duplicate-free parameter positions, unknown names reject at the declaration, and the clause requires the mutation bit only when an effect ceiling is declared. Callable types may bind parameter names in the same clause without changing signature identity. The compiler records inferred write footprints, mutation write facts and a sound return-origin bound, preserves them through copying, relocation, generic substitution, callable identity, interface schemas and bytecode validation, rejects forged or missing IR summaries, and versions the serialized effect contract as `effects-v3` so older entries are never admitted. M1 records these facts only; alias, overlap and ordinary call-permission enforcement is the separate M2 guarantee.
- [UNUSED-IMPORT-1] A direct `import` whose alias or dotted name no reference in its module resolves through is diagnosed as `W_UNUSED_IMPORT` at the imported name, beside `W_UNUSED_BINDING`. The evidence is the interface index's resolved-import flags, not an identifier scan: a qualified reference the checker accepted marks the import it resolved through, a binding that reused the first segment of a dotted path stays an ordinary value, and the alias inside a string literal or comment is not a reference. A cached body that was replayed rather than re-checked recovers its usage from the qualified names in its module's own source, so a warm run reports the same imports as a cold one. The warning is `W_*` and therefore rejecting under a configured `lint.warnings: "deny"` exactly like the other codes, and `import_policy` continues to evaluate every resolved direct import, used or not.
- [MUTATION-ENFORCE-1] Within a checked call, an input not listed in `mutates(...)` cannot have its reachable mutable objects written by that call or its transitive callees, and a function with no `mutation` effect cannot write caller-owned managed state. Origins propagate deeply through records, arrays, options, unions, collections, helper results, wrappers and shallow copies; definitely-fresh local storage stays writable; unknown is never fresh. A writable input graph that may overlap a read-only argument of a declared `mutates(...)` contract rejects with both argument sites unless proven disjoint, while two writable aliases are permitted; inferred writes of an unannotated callee are not a contract and carry no overlap obligation. Callee footprints substitute into actual arguments to a fixed point through recursion, unused generics, specializations and indirect callbacks; analysis-budget exhaustion widens conservatively with a located limitation. The guarantee is call-duration only and makes no total/trap-free/allocation-free/concurrency claim.
- [INCREMENTAL-SPEC-1] A generic specialization key mixes the template identity, canonical concrete arguments, the concrete provider type shape (fields, variants and layout flags), the target ABI and the compiler build identity; a provider-shape edit invalidates the key even when the template is unchanged.
- [INVALIDATION-EXECUTION-1] An invalidation report records actual query work: a stage a command did not run is reported `skipped`, never as reuse; lowering, emission and linking consume body and emitted-fragment inputs, so a body edit invalidates them; and a rejected check emits a best-effort explanation against the previous baseline without publishing a successful verdict.
- [WORK-REPORT-1] Ordinary check, build, run and session commands emit a versioned `work-report-v1` that separates an unchanged fingerprint from actual result reuse and reports each of parsed modules, checked bodies, instantiated specializations, lowered functions, emitted fragments, links and executed tests as `hit | miss | skipped` with fresh-performed and reused counts. A stage a command did not run is `skipped` and is never counted as reuse; bypass reasons and cumulative-versus-request session deltas are included. Existing `frontend-cache-v1` and `invalidation-report-v1` shapes remain comparison-only and are documented for migration.
- [INVALIDATION-BASELINE-1] A baseline is compared only after its version, implementation identity, inputs array and incremental module table validate; a structurally invalid baseline makes every executed stage miss with `corrupt-baseline` instead of comparing missing fields into hits.
- [CLI-2] Reports missing arguments and missing source files as usage or host failures.
- [EXIT-STATUS-1] The entry point's `int` result is the process exit status, the way a Python or Go program returns an exit code, truncated to the low 8 bits (POSIX `waitpid`: `status = value & 0xFF`), for `mylang run`, `run-project` and a native executable from `mylang build`. So an entry returning 7 exits 7 and one returning 256 exits 0. `--print-result` (in `MYLANG_OPTIONS` for a built binary) still writes the full value to standard output and is orthogonal to the status, so a program can print its result and exit with it at once. A runtime trap outranks the return and exits 1, because the program never reached it; source and check errors exit 1 and usage, capability and host failures exit 2. Unit and Result entries adapt first ([PYTHON-CORE-2]): a `unit` or `Ok(unit)` entry exits 0, an `Ok(n)` or `int` entry exits `n & 0xFF`, and an `Err(code)` entry exits `code & 0xFF`.
- [CLI-3] Executes the plan acceptance programs and recursive functions through the command-line executable.
- [CLI-4] Schema generation reads declared input manifests into generated modules on every invocation and rejects malformed or duplicate inputs. A generated module is ordinary source — a `#` provenance comment, a `module` line, `export` directives and one `def` per value — and spells a boolean `True`/`False` even though its manifest spells it the way JSON does.

- [CACHE-1] Reusing unchanged module syntax preserves fresh semantic checks and changed results, with independent returned storage and bounded cache retention.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-1] Ordinary one-shot `check`, `build`, `run` and `test` commands reuse parsed module syntax from a persistent content-addressed cache under `--cache-dir` (default `.mylang-cache/frontend`), keyed by the compiler build identity, the exact source path and bytes and the module body start. Declaration collection, symbol/export resolution, interface visibility, ownership, effects and every typed body still run fresh, and a changed byte yields a miss. Damaged, truncated or foreign entries fall back to a full parse, and `--no-cache` disables reuse.
- [CLI-5] Persistent checking sessions reuse unchanged parses and allow clearing retained cache storage between requests.
- [CLI-6] The removed C-backend command names `emit-c` and `emit-project-c` fail with a migration that names `emit-qbe` and `emit-project-qbe`; no live C backend or VM command remains in the CLI surface.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-2] An ordinary one-shot `check-project` may reuse a successful whole-program verdict from the persistent cache only after loading every module fresh and comparing each module name, path and source byte against the content-addressed key; reused warnings are replayed and interface/semantic work is skipped. Failed checks are never stored, a compiler or diagnostic-format change misses, and `--report`/`--timings` expose reuse and miss counts.
- [CACHE-CHECKSUM-1] Persisted frontend syntax and verdict entries and checked-body store entries carry a 64-bit payload checksum taken from the leading bytes of the payload's BLAKE3 digest; an entry whose payload no longer matches it is damaged and treated as a miss.
- [FRONTEND-CACHE-3] Cached module syntax round-trips every field of the parsed program, including expression lambdas, so a warm lookup checks the same program a cold parse would.
- [CHECK-SCALE-LEX-1] Reserved words and removed spellings are recognised through compile-time maps, layout lowering finds line starts in one forward pass over the source, header discovery lexes each module into a scratch arena it resets before the next module (keeping only its diagnostics and import facts) and records which imports a dotted reference qualifies from the tokens it already holds (so the unused-import warning never lexes a module twice), and a reference spelling made only of identifier bytes and dots is normalized without lexing it. Every spelling lexes to the same tokens and every reference resolves exactly as through the full lexer: a keyword segment is still not a name, and a removed spelling is still left to the lexer, which reports `E_REMOVED_SPELLING`.
- [CHECK-SUMMARY-1] A successful one-shot `check` of a project records one summary per ordinary function — its stable declaration key, dependency context, the BLAKE3 digest of its declaration region, its propagated effect mask and mutation summary, the diagnostics it contributed with region-relative spans, and its direct callees — in one slot per entry of the frontend cache. The next `check` of that entry stands a summary in for every body whose context and region are unchanged: the body is neither checked nor copied, the effect and mutation passes seed it with its stored facts and report nothing of their own for it, and its stored diagnostics are replayed at the region's current offset. The entry point, constants, conversions, host and foreign declarations, and bodies whose checked tree names a specialization, a lambda or a type interned while checking are always checked. After the passes every checked body that had a summary must still have exactly its stored effect mask and mutation summary, and no call cycle may pass through both a checked and a stood-in body; a changed fact, such a cycle, or any error the passes report makes the check repeat in full with no stand-ins, so a check's diagnostics are always those of a check without summaries. `work-report-v1` counts a stood-in body as a reused `checked_bodies` body; `--explain-invalidation`, `--no-cache` and every command other than `check` use no summaries.
- [CHECK-SUMMARY-2] A command that lowers the whole checked project (`build`'s frontend `emit-project-qbe`, `run`, `run-project`) reads the same summaries but checks every body, because lowering needs its tree; a summary stands in only for an unchanged body's effect and mutation facts and its pass diagnostics, so the effect and mutation passes walk only changed bodies. The same fact and cycle conditions as `check` apply, and a violated one repeats the passes in full. Such a command publishes summaries only when it had none to stand in or repeated in full, and a request served by summaries neither reads nor publishes retained checked trees.
- [SEMANTIC-KEY-IDENTITY-1] A declaration's dependency fingerprint names its defining module and, for a function, its `@deprecated` reason. Retargeting an import alias to another module's byte-identical declaration therefore invalidates every body that references it (the new target has its own types and inferred facts), and deprecating a callee invalidates its cross-module callers, which then report `W_DEPRECATED` exactly as a cold check does.
- [SEMANTIC-KEY-TRANSITIVE-1] A declaration's dependency fingerprint is transitive and independent of the order declarations are keyed in: it covers its own digest and the own digest of every declaration it reaches, so a cycle of recursive or mutually recursive types folds every member into every member. A composite type spelling (`list[T]`, `dict[K, V]`, `Option[T]`, tuples, arrays, callback types) references each of its component types, a type declaration's own digest frames its source text (field types, defaults, `record`/`class`), and a name a facade re-exports binds the declaration it names. A field renamed, retyped or reshaped in a record reached only through a list element, a dictionary value, an Option payload, a return type, a type cycle or a facade therefore invalidates every body that reaches it, and an incremental check reports what a cold check reports.
- [CHECK-SHAPES-1] A check summary, a retained checked tree and a retained specialization also record the transitive shape digest (schema of the record and of every record its fields, cases, elements and callback slots reach) of every catalog record the checked tree touched. A summary is not stood in, and a retained tree is not reused (`record-shape-changed`), unless every such record still exists under its name with the same shape in the fresh catalog, however the body reached it. The checked-body store format is `semantic-cache-v2` and the summary format `check-summaries-v3`.
- [CHECK-SCALE-KEYS-1] Dependency-key preparation records, per function, the BLAKE3 digest of its declaration region and the names its syntax references; check summaries keep both. While a function's region digest is unchanged, the next preparation resolves the recorded names instead of walking the function's syntax again, which binds exactly the declarations and negative lookups the walk would, so every dependency digest is unchanged; a digest that no longer matches is ignored and the syntax is walked.
- [INVALIDATION-REPORT-1] A checked project can be explained stage by stage against the previous `project-dependencies-v1` receipt: `--explain-invalidation` emits one `invalidation-report-v1` JSON line naming load, parse, declaration, interface, body, specialization, lowering, fragment and link hits and misses with the first changed dependency, a missing baseline is all-miss, a corrupt baseline is all-miss rather than a failure, and `--report` adds the same per-stage counts to `frontend-cache-v1`. The baseline lives under `<cache-dir>/invalidation/` and is not partitioned by compiler identity, so a compiler change can be explained.
- [INCREMENTAL-KEYS-1] Every incremental cache entry includes the compiler build identity, bootstrap-language version, target, runtime ABI, effects/capabilities contract and relevant flags through one contract fingerprint. Changing any dimension invalidates parsed syntax, whole-program verdicts, checked function bodies, generic-specialization keys and native object fragments even when the source bytes are identical.
- [INVALIDATION-MATRIX-1] For whitespace, comments, private bodies, exported signatures, effects, constants, record/union shape, imports, generic templates, specialization arguments, runtime ABI and compiler version, a warm check equals a clean rebuild in accepted status, diagnostic JSON, checked interface manifest, normalized QBE and reuse counts; missing or damaged cache data recomputes instead of trusting a partial entry.








- [NOMINAL-TYPE-SYNTAX-1] Nominal declarations retain explicit type parameters and requirements; nested named applications retain base and argument spans through parsing, formatting, syntax caching and module relocation. Lists are bounded at 16 and nesting at 256; module interfaces check exported type privacy, constructor kind, application arity and parameter shadowing. Malformed nominal declarations follow NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-CHECK-1; concrete specialization follows NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1.
- [GENERIC-SYNTAX-1] Generic syntax retains named type parameters, nonempty explicit requirement lists, and explicit `[...]` call type arguments through owned AST copying and formatting; duplicate parameters/requirements and lists beyond 16 are rejected. A specialized instance is named `f[i64]` after the arguments it was given, so a diagnostic and a disassembly name it the way its call site is written. Executable specialization must preserve these annotations through contract validation before lowering.

- [NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1] Concrete nominal records, unions, results and enums specialize by declaration and ordered semantic argument identities into complete runtime schemas. Construction, assignment, matching, result handling and owned-field flow use the ordinary checked IR/VM rules; concrete types compose with existing generic functions and numeric arrays. Unused templates and unexpanded phantom arguments do not become empty runtime records. Public type lookup requires exported bases and visible arguments, including phantom arguments. Symbolic nominal uses in generic function bodies follow NOMINAL-BODY-1. Canonical type display names have a 1 MiB compilation budget, and schema export of a generic type constructor remains explicitly unsupported pending generic tooling.
- [NOMINAL-LAYOUT-1] Nominal declarations, including unused templates, have bounded finite storage graphs before executable specialization. Canonical symbolic identities preserve declaration, parameter scope and ordered arguments. Only stored fields/payloads and array elements expand; phantom arguments do not create storage edges. Even zero-length arrays retain the existing finite-type rule. Cycles reject with E_RECURSIVE_RECORD and original closing-edge/related locations; growing families reject with E_GENERIC_LIMIT at 4096 nominal instances, 4096 array shapes or 100000 expansion operations. Graph IDs are not runtime type IDs and do not authorize execution.
- [NOMINAL-TEMPLATE-CHECK-1] Nominal declaration metadata resolves field and payload expressions to declaration identities and local parameters even in unused templates. It rejects unknown types, shadowing, duplicate members, malformed result schemas, application arity/kind errors and insufficient argument requirements. Copy guarantees propagate through ordinary and generic records/unions without copying owners or pending results; scalar domains and enums retain equality without integer operations. Expression checking is bounded at 256 levels and 100000 visits. Metadata is not an executable layout: finite storage dependencies follow NOMINAL-LAYOUT-1; concrete instances follow NOMINAL-INSTANCE-1; symbolic nominal operations in generic function bodies follow NOMINAL-BODY-1.
- [GENERIC-REQUIREMENTS-1] Builtin generic requirements are copy, equality, and integer, with integer implying equality and equality implying copy. Matching uses concrete equality rules and forbids implicit copying of owners or pending results; nominal integer-backed domains are not integers. Unknown requirement names and builtin type-variable shadowing are diagnosed even in uncalled templates before specialization.

- [GENERIC-BODY-1] The initial abstract generic body checker keeps type parameters distinct and requires declared integer/equality support for the corresponding operations. It checks pure builtin/type-parameter signatures, locals, returns, branches, loops, and casts without substituting representative concrete types. Unsupported forms and 256-depth/100000-work bounds reject explicitly; concrete specialization must retain these universal checks.

- [GENERIC-BODY-2] The abstract generic body checker applies the same wrapping conversion rule to `?` as the concrete path: when a propagated result's error type is not already the enclosing function's error type but is the payload of a case of that enclosing union, the propagation is accepted and the error is wrapped in that case; a symbolic union case payload compares structurally, so distinct type parameters still stay distinct.

- [GENERIC-ARRAY-1] Fixed numeric arrays may contain generic type parameters in signatures, constructors, annotations, nested arrays and explicit type arguments. Universal checking retains element requirements, exact lengths, nominal identities, copy/result obligations and source-located traps before specialization. Concrete shapes are interned by element identity and length, with bounded specialization; symbolic enum-keyed tables and parameterized lengths remain explicitly unsupported.
- [GENERIC-CALL-1] Abstract calls between pure generic templates resolve declaration identities and domain/module privacy, require complete explicit or uniquely inferred type arguments and value-argument arity, enforce requirement implication, and substitute parameter/result identities without merging distinct type variables. Recursive calls check signatures without expanding bodies; reachable concrete helpers must also pass the abstract pure-body rules.

- [GENERIC-HELPER-1] Abstract generic checking validates every transitively called ordinary helper against the supported pure builtin body rules. A bounded declaration worklist checks each reachable body once, including recursive cycles; uncalled ordinary bodies are left to concrete checking. Helper signatures alone cannot authorize hidden effects. Broader effect and ownership contracts remain unfinished.

- [GENERIC-INSTANCE-1] The compilation-local specialization registry interns resolved declaration identities and ordered concrete type identities, owns argument storage, preserves IDs across growth and allocation failure, and reuses recursive requests. It bounds discovery at 4096 entries and each argument list at 16. Nominal types remain distinct regardless of representation. Executable checking uses these identities without conflating them with source declaration ordinals.

- [GENERIC-EXECUTION-1] Pure generic templates specialize on explicit or uniquely inferred concrete type arguments after universal body validation. Repeated and recursive calls reuse instances; per-instance bindings, expression resolution, widths and nominal types remain distinct. Ordinary entry/export wrappers execute instances through the checked native backend. Internal instances are excluded from direct module export/schema roots. Main cannot be generic; unsupported abstract forms still reject.





- [GENERIC-DIAGNOSTIC-1] Checked specializations retain explicit ordered concrete type arguments. Human-readable function names include those types, preserve qualified nominal names, and do not depend on discovery ordinals. Traces and reports use these names, while public-root selection uses specialization metadata rather than spelling.

## Named constraints

- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SYNTAX-1] A top-level `constraint NAME[SUBJECT] = REQUIREMENTS` names one subject binder whose right-hand side reuses the `+` requirement grammar. The parser rejects non-bare subjects, applied bounds such as `Prices[int]`, named or self references on the declaration's right-hand side, and requirement lists beyond the existing limit; the declaration and its bound references survive owned AST copying, relocation, formatting, declaration listing, and module-interface checks.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-1] Constraint declarations participate in the module declaration namespace and explicit exports with a distinct kind, resolve before signatures are checked, and accept bare and `alias.Name` function type-parameter bounds; an exported generic signature may use an exported constraint.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-2] Named bounds only apply to function type parameters. Records, unions, domains and constant-length parameters reject them, and a constraint name is not a value type: `policy: Prices` and `Prices[int]` report a declaration-kind error.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-3] Importing or naming a private constraint from another module rejects with the existing privacy diagnostic, an exported generic whose named bound is not exported rejects with `E_PRIVATE_INTERFACE`, and an undefined bound name reports `E_GENERIC_REQUIREMENT` at the bound.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-4] Constraint declarations obey existing namespace rules: a collision with another declaration or an import alias reports `E_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` or `E_IMPORT_ALIAS`.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-5] Resolving a constraint identity outside the mapped project source is an internal error rather than a silently wrong declaration.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-6] A constraint subject may not shadow a module declaration, a direct import alias, or a builtin type, and an exported constraint's nested requirement signature may not expose private types.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-MODULE-7] Same-module and imported exported constraints resolve to the same declaration identity through the ordinary direct-import and alias rules, so interface and semantic checking cannot disagree.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-CACHE-1] Constraint declarations and their named bound references round trip through the persistent parsed-syntax cache under the bumped frontend format version; the in-memory syntax cache copies them with the rest of the declaration tables.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-1] A named bound expands to the same builtin/field/operation contract as the equivalent inline bound, so generic calls, concrete conformance and forwarding accept and reject the same operations in both spellings, and independently declared names with equivalent requirements satisfy the same obligations.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-2] The declaration's subject substitutes by type-parameter identity recursively through supported composite signature forms, so an operation requiring `list[P]` accepts the provider's `list[Provider]` without rewriting source text or capturing a consumer's same-spelled name.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-3] Required operation effect ceilings compare as before: a concrete method whose declared effects are a subset is accepted, one that exceeds the ceiling rejects with `E_GENERIC_ARGUMENT`, and callers are checked against the full required ceiling.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-4] Forwarding uses the existing implication rules: a stronger named bound may forward to a weaker one, and a weaker bound cannot forward to a stronger one even when the forwarding function is never called. Named and inline forms behave identically.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-5] Expansion counts unique explicitly required members and builtins, including implied guarantees, before applying the existing 16-requirement limit, and reports `E_GENERIC_LIMIT` when the expanded contract exceeds it.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-6] Identical members from distinct bundles or from a bundle and an inline list coalesce; a repeated same-name field or operation with a different resolved type or effect ceiling rejects with `E_GENERIC_REQUIREMENT` and `E_PRIVATE_INTERFACE`-style related locations, and repeating one resolved declaration through two aliases rejects as a duplicate.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SEMANTICS-7] Unused constraint declarations are still checked: an invalid subject, an unknown or shadowed name, a nested named reference, a structural field without `copy`, or an unknown requirement signature type rejects before any provider or call exists.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-FIXTURE-1] The acceptance fixture checks and tests its fake-only `fake_tests.my` entry successfully with `production.my` absent and with an invalid unimported `production.my`, and the production entry still rejects the invalid imported body once `main.my` imports it.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-1] Standalone programs reject duplicate `constraint` declarations with a located duplicate-symbol diagnostic, whether or not either declaration is used, so adding a module header does not change declaration-namespace rules.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-2] Standalone constraint names collide with records, variants, results, enums, domains, resources, type aliases and top-level functions in either declaration order, while non-constraint duplicate collisions keep their existing diagnostics.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-3] Standalone constraint lookup reports the duplicate-symbol diagnostic instead of silently selecting the first of several same-named declarations.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-NAMESPACE-4] Standalone valid unused and multi-constraint programs remain accepted.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-SUBJECT-1] Standalone constraint subjects obey the same declaration-shadowing rules as module subjects, including unused declarations and collisions with types, aliases, functions and constraint names in either source order. Builtin type names remain invalid subjects; independent constraints may reuse a subject binder.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-CALLBACK-1] Structural requirement normalization resolves generic callback parameters and results recursively, preserving nested type identities and callback effect ceilings. Equivalent named and inline callback requirements coalesce and forward; mismatched parameter, result or effect contracts reject, and concrete callback aliases keep the same identity as their expanded signature.
- [NAMED-CONSTRAINT-REVIEW-1] A permanent regression suite drives the review repros through the compiler: imported exported-constraint identity reads the defining declaration's symbol table; concrete names inside a named declaration resolve in that declaration's scope and cannot be captured by a same-spelled consumer parameter; merging compares resolved member identities and subject positions rather than source spelling; implied and named `copy` guarantees reach module preflight; the expanded requirement limit counts unique resolved members; checked schemas publish exported constraints, named bindings and expanded promises; and standalone declarations enforce namespace and full structural validity even when unused.








- [SOURCE-CODEC-1] Domain codec declarations remain specialist metadata, while the removed `codec_encode` and `codec_decode` source spellings report `E_REMOVED_SPELLING` and direct callers to the documented domain codec API.

- [CHECK-CACHE-1] A persistent checking session may reuse a successful verdict only after fresh project and manifest loading and exact comparison of every loaded module name, path and source byte. Hits replay owned warnings and skip interface/semantic work; failures are never retained. Retention is bounded and clear drops syntax and verdict caches while preserving cumulative observations.

- [GENERIC-CONCRETE-1] Abstract generic checking resolves concrete copyable nominal and fixed-array schemas without conflating them with type parameters. Concrete record/array construction and projection obey declared field, element and index types; malformed uncalled templates reject. Concrete owned flow follows GENERIC-OWNED-1; individual runtime operations require their own abstract rules. Concrete result obligations follow GENERIC-RESULT-1.

- [GENERIC-UPDATE-1] Generic bodies may update concrete copyable records and replace fixed-array/table elements. Field uniqueness and field/element/index types are checked before instantiation; normal specialization preserves immutable snapshots, evaluation order and runtime bounds. These operations do not infer structural capabilities for symbolic parameters.

- [GENERIC-VARIANT-1] Abstract generic checking supports construction and exhaustive matching of concrete copyable enums/unions. Cases retain nominal identities and exact payload types; each arm scopes its payload independently, duplicate/missing cases reject, and a match terminates only when every arm terminates. Named enum-table construction requires every key exactly once. Result-bearing variants follow GENERIC-RESULT-1; runtime-owned value flow follows GENERIC-OWNED-1.

- [GENERIC-RESULT-1] Abstract checking of concrete result-bearing generic values enforces single transfer, explicit handling, scope/return obligations, and agreement across continuing branches, match arms, loops and short-circuit paths before instantiation. Nested payloads retain obligations and aggregate operations cannot erase siblings or replaced results. Propagation requires compatible concrete result errors and rejects live result bindings or previously evaluated result temporaries on its early-return path. An explicit `_ =` release evaluates its operand. Symbolic result type constructors remain pending.



- [GENERIC-CLEANUP-1] Concrete resource ownership passed through generic functions retains its provider lifecycle. Explicit partial extraction/drop and implicit scope cleanup release each live resource exactly once, including allocation failures and traps; forwarding does not complete or duplicate an external resource.


- [GENERIC-PROVIDER-1] Abstract generic provider operations enforce concrete or symbolic nominal result contracts, exact capability kinds and fixed byte-array inputs before instantiation. Resource I/O requires a mutable unconsumed owner after argument evaluation. Acquisition/completion preserve resource ownership and permission attenuation cannot create authority. Direct clock/random/environment/filesystem/network effects propagate through helper calls and enforce bounds even in uncalled or unreachable source paths.

- [GENERIC-PROVIDER-EXEC-1] Generic provider execution preserves ordinary callback values/counts, bounded output bytes, typed failures, retry ownership and cleanup under allocation failure.
