Short-circuit paths remain unevaluated at runtime while their possible effects remain statically checked.






- [DIAGNOSTIC-JSON-1] CLI --diagnostics=json selects diagnostic-v1 JSON Lines on stderr without changing command results or exit statuses. Source and VM diagnostics preserve stable codes, severity, original-file byte ranges and byte-based line/columns, related/origin/requirement notes, bounded frames and omitted-frame counts. Project-loader related sites retain their own module coordinates. Auxiliary CLI messages are separate message records. Successful check-session warning replay uses the selected format, and cache identity prevents cross-format replay. Default text remains available; invalid command-line syntax and emergency output failures may use text.


- [HOST-SOURCE-1] Contextual host fn declarations have no guest body and require an explicit effects(host, ...) contract. Only ordinary top-level, non-generic copied data signatures are supported; capabilities, managed collections and runtime-owned graphs reject. Main retains a guest body. Host and additional declared effects propagate through ordinary and abstract generic calls, including uncalled templates. Effect-free evaluation/invocation cannot execute these calls. Modules, formatting and disassembly preserve declarations; duplicate discovery excludes external implementations.


- [BOARD-TRIAL-1] The bounded board-validation experiment runs its guest as a native executable with a trusted geometry provider linked into it, compares deterministic reports and callback counts with an independent fixed oracle and standalone Zig implementation, and releases snapshot and callback graphs on allocation failure.

- [NOMINAL-BODY-1] Generic bodies retain nominal declaration identity and ordered symbolic arguments, check fields and payloads before calls, and preserve construction, assignment, exhaustive matches, named result propagation, partial ownership and cleanup effects. Substitution and late VM catalog materialization retain stable type IDs and reuse existing numeric array shapes. Phantom arguments retain identity without contributing stored-value contracts. Shape traversal uses an explicit stack and the generic checking work budget; argument-growing runtime specialization remains bounded by the nominal graph/name limits. Ordinary unconstrained type parameters still require structural constraints for member operations. Provider-specific symbolic shapes follow NOMINAL-PROVIDER-1; public generic schema tooling remains pending.

- [NOMINAL-PROVIDER-1] Generic resource/environment operations accept symbolic nominal result and payload records only when their exact named fields satisfy the ordinary provider contract for every permitted substitution. Declaration order does not affect meaning. Byte inputs and output arrays must have known u8 elements and numeric lengths; a general integer parameter is insufficient. Concrete and symbolic checking share named contract definitions. Resource identity, authority, mutable access, handling, cleanup and effect checks remain enforced before calls. VM specialization preserves bounded bytes, absent environment values, partial I/O, failed-completion ownership and cleanup under allocation failure or instruction traps.

- [GENERIC-CODEC-1] Generic domain codec metadata preserves exact value types, result obligations, transitive effects, declaration privacy and module relocation for tooling. The former `codec_encode`/`codec_decode` source entry points are removed; `wrap`/`unwrap` still require the exact declaring domain and private methods remain inaccessible outside it.

- [BOARD-ABSTRACTION-1] The board trial uses a shared Slot<T:copy> for optional IDs, regions, features and first violations, and one equality-constrained membership helper for component/net IDs. Typed snapshots preserve each instantiated slot's exact payload identity; a NetId payload cannot enter a component slot. Consolidation preserves the fixed board contract, oracle results, callback counts and repair fixture failure/restoration behavior.

- [GENERIC-BODY-3] A generic body, and every non-generic function it calls, may sort a concrete list in place with `values.sort()` when the element is an integer, a float or `str`, the same set the concrete checker accepts: the abstract checker types the call as unit and records it as a write to its receiver, as it does `append`, but seeds no allocation, because a sort allocates nothing managed. A list whose element is a type parameter or any other type still rejects with `E_COLLECTION_METHOD`, and every other unsupported collection operation keeps `E_GENERIC_BODY_UNSUPPORTED`.
- [GENERIC-BODY-4] A generic body may sort any list in place with `values.sort(key=key, reverse=flag)`, on a named receiver or any expression: the abstract checker checks the operands in source order, `reverse` as a `bool`, records a write to the receiver and seeds `managed_allocation` for the key list, and leaves the key to each specialization, which checks it against its concrete element type under LIST-SORT-KEY-2. Without `key=` the element still needs a native order, as for GENERIC-BODY-3, and a positional or unknown operand is `E_CALL_ARGUMENT`. A generic body may also call the prelude `sorted(values, key=..., reverse=...)` when no binding or declaration claims the name, typed as its list argument, with the same operand rules and `managed_allocation`, which is how `sort.sort_by_key` forwards; and it may take `values.copy()` of a list, typed as the list and seeding `managed_allocation`.
- [GENERIC-LAMBDA-1] A lambda written inside a generic function body is `E_GENERIC_BODY_UNSUPPORTED` at the lambda, reported once, with a message that names the fix (declare a top-level function and pass it by name): a lambda is one synthesized function with one callback type, so it cannot follow the template's type parameters into each specialization. This holds for a sort key (`sorted(values, key=lambda v: ...)`, `values.sort(key=...)`) and for any other callback argument, including one whose type mentions no type parameter; it replaces the earlier pair of an unknown-name error and a missing-callback-context error. A named function key in a generic body still sorts under GENERIC-BODY-4.

## Interpreted schematic pilot

- [SCHEMATIC-1] The guest S-expression parser, circuit model, interface expansion and SVG renderer execute in the bytecode VM. A typed scalar host boundary exposes only bounded UTF-8 source bytes and unpublished output. Invalid inputs, unsupported forms, incomplete or duplicate pads and invalid ports reject without publishing partial SVG. The pilot supports one parameterless module with one 41-pad IC and supported two-terminal primitives; its documented limits and metadata exclusions are not a general Netlisp evaluator or electrical sign-off.


- [NAVIGATION-1] Compiler navigation indexes module-qualified declaration identities, original source ranges, signatures, effects, concrete call/read/write/field/type relationships and bounded source excerpts without VM execution. Every result carries compiler and loaded-source identities. Static relationships do not establish runtime coverage. Failed body checking emits declarations without checked relationships; lexical recovery after body syntax errors emits explicitly unchecked candidates.
- [GENERIC-API-1] Checked public schemas expose generic templates separately from concrete callable functions and data types, preserving parameter requirements, symbolic signatures, fields/cases, module import scope, effect contracts and source identity. Templates require explicit specialization and cannot be invoked as concrete values through the JSON API. Exported constraint declarations are published as non-callable `constraint` templates, and an exported generic parameter exposes each named bound's spelling and declaring module together with the expanded builtin/field/operation promises it contributes, under the bumped `generic-templates-v2` version.
- [GENERIC-API-DEFAULT-COPY-1] Public generic schemas retain the implicit `copy` requirement of unbounded function and nominal type parameters, while explicit `storable` or `codec` bounds do not acquire `copy`.
- [GENERIC-API-SUBJECT-1] Expanded named requirements in public schemas substitute only parsed references to the declaration's subject, recursively through nominal applications, arrays and callback signatures. Qualified concrete names with the subject's spelling retain their identity in both the combined contract and the per-bound expansion.
- [STRUCTURAL-FIELD-1] Copy-constrained function parameters may require named fields with exact named types, including another declared type parameter. Concrete satisfaction and symbolic forwarding preserve nominal identity and checked field reads/updates. Structural fields do not expose opaque domain backing or permit incomplete construction of an unknown record.
- [STRUCTURAL-OP-1] Function parameters may require public nongeneric domain operations with explicit parameter/result types and an effect ceiling. Type-owned generic calls check declared contracts before specialization; concrete operations must satisfy signature, visibility and effects.
- [STRUCTURAL-LIMIT-1] Structural requirements currently apply to function templates with copy guarantees and named type expressions. Nominal type declarations and unknown complete constructors reject explicitly.
- [INCREMENTAL-CAPACITY-1] The syntax cache retains at most 256 modules within its unchanged byte budget; edits can reuse exact unchanged module syntax while complete semantic checking and diagnostic equivalence remain required.


### Disk application raw byte host

- [BYTE-IO-1] The raw byte host preserves arbitrary input, enforces bounds and publishes output only on successful guest exit. Its operations are a trusted native provider compiled into the guest's own executable, which buffers output where only the driver can publish it.

The `mylang-io --project DIRECTORY` host compiles `main.my` and its imports from
disk into bytecode. It supplies only `io::length() -> i64`,
`io::byte(index: i64) -> u8`, and `io::put(value: u8) -> i64`, each with host effects.
Input bytes are passed untouched, including invalid UTF-8 and NUL. The guest owns
parsing, validation, algorithms, and serialization. Input is bounded at 2 MiB;
output at 128 MiB; a put returns zero on success. Negative/out-of-range reads and
output exhaustion fail the host call. Output is published only after guest main
returns zero, never after traps or nonzero exits. The host supplies no external
filesystem, network, environment or process authority to the guest. Host calls/work
are bounded at 300 million each and guest execution at 2 billion steps; the coordinator
also enforces wall/CPU/memory limits. The executable is built outside application
sessions; editing guest files requires no Zig rebuild or language Guardian run.

- [STANDARD-MODULES-1] Reserved standard sources load identically through all project providers and participate in source budgets, dependency graphs and digests. They are ordinary Python-syntax guest modules over managed types and may import one another.
- [STANDARD-BYTE-BUILDER-1] The standard `byte_builder` module assembles an immutable `bytes` snapshot over one mutable `byte_buffer` ([PY-BYTES-6]), so `append`, `append_range`, `append_byte`, `append_spaces`, `append_text`, `append_int` and `append_float` write each fragment's octets straight into that buffer's capacity and no fragment allocates a value for itself, with `append_float` writing the canonical spelling `str(value)` prints. The checked appends reject before writing anything, so a refused call leaves the builder byte-for-byte unchanged: `append_range` rejects a negative `start`, an `end` below `start` or an `end` past `len(data)` and accepts an empty range that changes nothing, `append_byte` rejects values outside `0..255`, and `append_spaces` and `reserve` reject negative counts. `reserve` grows capacity for a further `extra` octets without changing the finished length, and `finish` publishes the buffer as an immutable snapshot in append order without copying its octets ([PY-BYTES-SNAPSHOT-1]): it contributes `managed_allocation` but no mutation, the builder stays usable, and later appends, including an append of a snapshot back into its own builder, cannot change an earlier result. Every operation keeps the module's published names, argument types, effects and results, carries `mutation` and `managed_allocation` where it writes, mutates only its builder argument, and leaves the buffer traced, so a builder survives collection at every managed allocation.

- [PLAIN-JSON-1] The strict document parser produces a checked i64 tape for a JSON text. It rejects duplicate decoded keys, invalid Unicode, integers outside i64, nonfinite numbers, trailing data and container depth over 64. Its byte budget is at most 1 MiB; scratch and output allocation remain bounded.
- [PLAIN-JSON-2] Ordinary JSON validation applies to every subtree, including unused fields: decoded duplicate keys, malformed UTF-8, out-of-range integers, nonfinite floats, trailing bytes and excessive nesting reject.
- [PLAIN-JSON-3] Failed document parsing releases partially allocated DOM, tape and text storage.
- [STANDARD-MODULES-2] Embedded standard source bytes count against the same per-file and total project source budgets as user modules.

## Guest application test tool

- [NATIVE-TEST-13] Successful guest tests with deterministic allocation or mutation confined to their fresh process and keyed inputs may reuse result receipts. Both checked-facts and emitted-code dependency metadata use the same classification; filesystem, network, clock, randomness, environment, host and output effects still prevent reuse. A declared mutation footprint still excludes signature-pure outcome replay.

- [NATIVE-TEST-12] Native guest-test cache identity ignores inherited environment variables the compiler/build does not read and source files outside the resolved import closure, while retaining compiler-affecting environment inputs, disabling durable reuse under dynamic-loader overrides or environmental capabilities, and invalidating on transitive dependency, project configuration/policy, provider, tool, or resolution-affecting candidate changes. A run still materializes a broad immutable source snapshot, but successful receipt publication verifies the same resolved identity so an unrelated neighboring edit does not fail with SourceChanged.

- [NATIVE-RUN-MANIFEST-CACHE-1] After a project graph has been loaded, policy-validated, and source-checked, run-project may reuse a checksum-valid emitted native manifest keyed by the ordered resolved source graph, compiler identity, policy identity, and emission controls; a warm hit performs zero lowering/emission, while --no-cache, changed inputs, or corrupt entries force fresh emission.

- [NATIVE-TEST-1] `mylang test` checks the project and discovers `test_`-prefixed declarations: every exported one, and an unexported one only when it already has the defaulting shape — no generic parameters, no value parameters, and a `bool` result — so a private helper named `test_` never aborts a run. A discovered test whose shape cannot default (value parameters without `arguments`, or a non-`bool` result without `expected`) is reported as one failed case rather than a host failure. It validates named typed JSON inputs and compares native results structurally. Filters and reasoned skips never turn an empty or all-skipped selection into success. Each execution uses a fresh bounded native process without implicit host grants.
- [NATIVE-TEST-2] Successful deterministic tests may reuse verified receipts keyed by semantic dependency closure, call signature, relevant layouts, case settings, execution limits, and tool/runtime identity. That identity follows QBE-4: the compiler's embedded build-time digest, and path, size and modification time for external tools, rather than rereading executables on every run. Whitespace and unrelated body edits preserve valid results; affected helper edits invalidate consumers. Corrupt receipts are misses; failed executions and executions with an external effect other than output ([NATIVE-TEST-OUTPUT-1]) are not reused. `--force` executes selected unskipped tests.
- [NATIVE-TEST-OUTPUT-1] Output is a deterministic effect for result reuse, like process-local mutation and allocation: a test that takes `out: Stream` or `err: Stream` and has no other external effect reuses its receipt under the same identity as any other test (semantic dependency closure, call signature, layouts, case settings, execution limits, collector stress and tool/runtime identity). The runner captures both streams of a test process and discards them when the test passes, and a receipt records only a pass, so a reused pass shows exactly what an executed pass shows; a failure, whose captured output is reported with it, always executes again. A test that also reads the clock, entropy, the environment, a file or the network remains uncacheable.
- [NATIVE-TEST-3] Provider fixtures replace only existing dependency sources in private bounded snapshots, preserve the selected test signature and exported types, and never edit originals. A source change observed during one invocation and any compiler change invalidate results before publication, but an unrelated source edit no longer invalidates an unaffected receipt: acceptance is bound to the selected test/provider dependency closure, contract identity and provider identity, with the resolved-source identity retained for during-invocation change detection and diagnostics; provider replacement additionally verifies the full captured inventory. Native reports distinguish passed, cached, failed, and skipped cases; setup failures exit 2 and empty/all-skipped selections exit 3.
- [NATIVE-TEST-4] A directory argument selects every project root beneath it in sorted order — a `.my` file that declares a top-level `main`, plus standalone `*_test.my` files — treats library modules imported by a root as part of that project rather than programs of their own (a library with tests that no root imports is selected too, NATIVE-TEST-14), skips generated trees, and runs one bounded child command per root across the machine's cores. Child reports merge into one case listing, one count summary and the most severe exit status, and children share the object cache and receipt directory so an unchanged program reuses its results instead of rebuilding. A filter carrying no `*`, `?` or `[` matches any substring of a qualified test name; one carrying them keeps shell-style wildcard semantics. A per-case configuration file is rejected for a directory run, because its keys name the tests of one project.
- [NATIVE-TEST-5] Before checking any production implementation body, native test preparation parses every reachable module and validates exported declarations and interfaces, then reads the case configuration and fully checks only the selected replacement provider graph. The declaration manifest carries canonical declaration-level type descriptors that normalize builtin aliases and expand transparent aliases while preserving nominal identities, and discovery and provider comparison consume those descriptors without checking unselected production bodies. Replacements are validated against every public contract they replace — names, defaults, canonical types and nominal identities, data shapes, generic requirements, declared effects, mutation footprints and return-origin bounds — using the directional subset relation, so a narrower implementation is accepted and a stronger one rejects. An unselected production body may contain a typed hole or body type error while its declarations remain valid; a production interface error, a malformed or unknown replacement, or a syntax error still rejects, and a full production build still rejects the unfinished body. Test receipts record the selected dependency-closure key plus the contract and provider identities, so changing a fake value or a relevant contract re-executes its case. Identical provider graphs share one declaration validation, checked project and prepared native library per invocation; distinct provider graphs stay isolated.
- [NATIVE-TEST-6] After a project validity check through the shared query cache, a warm unchanged deterministic native-test run looks up valid result receipts and executes zero tests without lowering, emitting or linking any code; only the validity check runs, with the declaration check when its contracts are not already cached (NATIVE-TEST-INTERFACES-1). Cached execution is admitted only with the closure-bound plan key, contract identity, provider identity and fixture/environment/budget identities, so a helper edit executes only its dependent tests and changed expectations, arguments, providers, environment, budgets or force mode invalidate exactly the affected receipts. A fully cached selection additionally requires durable checked facts (`native-test-graph-v3`) whose key covers compiler/tool stamps, mode, environment hash, source identity and canonical provider identity; a wrong or damaged graph is a miss. When facts or receipts miss, one checked compilation per selected provider graph produces the test facts and is retained: a later selective lowering request consumes that same checked result instead of re-checking the project in a second process, and any fallback recheck is reported as real checked-body work rather than hidden. Only the selected tests' reachable function closure is lowered, emitted and linked; callbacks, function values, codec boundaries and any indirect-call set widen conservatively rather than omitting required functions. Identical provider graphs share one prepared closure and library, while distinct graphs stay isolated.
- [PURE-SKIP-1] `mylang test --pure-skip` may replay a recorded pass-or-fail outcome for an unchanged test that is both effect-cacheable and signature-pure (no capability parameter and no declared `mutates(...)` footprint) without executing it and without depending on the positive-only receipt. The record is keyed by the same semantic dependency-closure key, contract identity and provider identity as the receipt, and additionally binds the expected value; a recorded failure replays as a failure with its message and never as a pass. A changed dependency, provider, contract or flag is a miss and re-runs, and `--force`, `--no-cache` and any non-pure test never take the path. Without the flag the default result is unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-7] `mylang test` runs each case with the guest's stdout redirected off the runner's binary protocol channel: the child captures bounded output and returns it in the response, the parent appends it to a failure's text, a passing case discards it, and a benchmarked function's output goes to the diagnostic stream. `print` inside a guest test therefore never aborts a run with `InvalidNativeTestResponse`.

- [NATIVE-TEST-8] A failed typed expectation reports both sides of the comparison as `expected <value>, got <value>` — a Boolean test shows its actual and expected values — while a trap keeps its located code and any captured guest output.

- [NATIVE-TEST-9] A guest test entry need not declare `main`: a library module or a standalone `*_test.my` file is checked through a private projection that appends a stub entry point, and a matching `module` header when the file has none, while the `main` requirement for ordinary `run` is unchanged. A standalone `*_test.my` beneath a subdirectory resolves its imports and dotted module name from the enclosing project root — the nearest ancestor holding `mylang.json`, else the nearest ancestor holding a non-test `.my` source — so `tests/parser_test.my` is module `tests.parser_test` and `import sexpr.parser` reads from the project root. A child compiler's located source diagnostic is surfaced alone with exit status 1 rather than wrapped in the generic host-failure line.
- [NATIVE-TEST-HEADERLESS-1] `mylang test` accepts every file that `check`, `run` and `build` accept: a headerless entry — with or without a `main` — is tested through a projection whose `module NAME;` header is written in front of the file's first line rather than on a line of its own, so every diagnostic, trap location and test failure reports the line number of the user's file. A headered entry with a `main` is tested unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-10] A failed `assert` reports the message written after its condition: the per-function native metadata carries one message per program counter, `mylang test` renders it as `R_ASSERT: <message>` in the failure text beside the located code and any captured output, and `mylang run` shows the same message on its human diagnostic; an assertion with no message keeps the bare `R_ASSERT`, and a message containing JSON syntax leaves the versioned trap record valid.
- [NATIVE-TEST-11] A directory run's merged summary counts the programs whose child produced no usable report — a build or other host failure — and names each one on stderr as `not run: <program> (exit <status>, no report)`. The count also travels in the JSON report as `dropped`, and a non-zero count keeps the existing setup-failure exit status, so a totals-only reading of `N passed, 0 failed` can never call a run green while whole programs are missing from it.
- [NATIVE-TEST-14] A directory run also selects a `.my` library that declares a test — a `def test_` function, public or not, or an `@test` function — when no selected root's import closure reaches it, and checks it through the stub-`main` projection of NATIVE-TEST-9. Reachability is the compiler's own module-header graph from each root's project root, so a library a root imports keeps running once, as part of that root; of two such libraries where one imports the other, only the importer is selected. A test file therefore needs neither `main` nor the `*_test.my` suffix to run.
- [NATIVE-TEST-15] Every child report lists, as `modules`, the absolute paths of the project sources its check loaded. After merging, a directory run names each test-declaring source beneath the directory that no reporting child loaded on stderr as `not run: <path> (declares tests, but no program loaded it)`, counts them as `unloaded` in the JSON report (a dropped program is already named by NATIVE-TEST-11 and is not counted again) and as `N test sources not loaded` on the count line, and exits with the setup-failure status 2, so a test file can never drop out of a run silently.
- [AFFECTED-TEST-1] `mylang test --affected --against REF` derives its selection from the working-tree diff rather than from the standing tree: it checks the project, maps each declaration's merged source span back to its module file and original line range, intersects that range with the changed lines `git` reports for `REF`, and runs a discovered test only when a changed declaration is reachable from it over the resolved call graph. It is a selection and never a verdict: the selected count and every skipped test are printed, `--filter` still applies first, `lint --gate` and CI run the whole set, and an empty derivation exits 3 exactly as a filter matching nothing does. `--against` without `--affected` (and `--affected` without `--against`) is a usage error, and a `REF` `git` cannot resolve is a host failure (status 2) rather than an empty selection. A project that cannot be checked for any other reason runs the whole discovered set instead of silently skipping tests.
- [GUEST-TEST-GC-STRESS-1] `mylang test --gc-stress` runs every collected test's copied-value call with the runtime's stress collector, the same collector coverage `native_execute` gets from its own `--gc-stress` argument, and a directory run forwards the flag to every child so one invocation reports one collector condition. The flag adds no expected value of its own, is part of the execution identity a receipt binds, and without it the collector is not stressed and the default result is unchanged.

- [NATIVE-TEST-SCALE-1] A `mylang test` run's work follows the edit, not the project. After a body edit, exactly the tests whose semantic dependency closure contains the edited function are lowered, linked and executed — the test library holds only those tests' closures, and every other test is answered from its receipt — and an edit that no test's closure reaches builds and executes nothing. An unchanged rerun starts exactly one compiler child, the validity check, which replays the verdict the previous run's checked-compilation session recorded; it runs no declaration check, facts session, build or harness compile.
- [NATIVE-TEST-PARALLEL-1] Selected test cases execute concurrently: `--jobs N`, then `MYLANG_JOBS`, then the cores the run may use capped by how many per-test memory ceilings free memory holds, sets how many worker threads each start one fresh bounded runner process at a time without copying the parent (`clone(CLONE_VM | CLONE_VFORK)`), and each child answers into its own response file (`--response FILE`). Every outcome — a value, a trap, `Timeout` for a child still running at `--timeout-ms`, or an abnormal exit with its stderr — lands in its case's slot, so a report never depends on completion order. A directory run gives each child an equal share of its worker width.
- [NATIVE-TEST-RUNNER-1] The compiled POSIX test harness is published once under `<cache>/runner/<key>`, keyed by every embedded harness source byte, its compile flags and the `zig` tool stamp, and later runs reuse it instead of compiling it again; a `--no-cache` run compiles a private copy.
- [NATIVE-TEST-STABLE-1] A run materializes its source snapshot at a path that is the same on every run of one project from one cache — `$TMPDIR/.pending/snapshot-<key>`, claimed through the scratch owner lock and cleared of whatever a killed run left — so the compiler's path-keyed caches (the check verdict, parsed modules, emitted-fragment packs) answer across runs; a run that cannot claim it stages at a fresh private path as before, and every run removes its snapshot on exit. The `native-test-compile` session records the `check-project` verdict of the project it checked, unless a configured `warnings: "deny"` rejects that project, so the next unchanged run's validity check replays it.
- [NATIVE-TEST-INTERFACES-1] The production declaration check (`list-interfaces --contracts`) is answered from `<cache>/interfaces/` when the same resolved source identity, configuration, compiler and compiler-affecting environment produced a clean answer before: exit status 0 and nothing on stderr but `--report` records. Each test entry keeps one slot, the latest clean answer behind the identity it answers for, so the store does not grow with edits. A warning, a refusal, `--force` and `--no-cache` always run the check, and a damaged entry is a miss.
- [NATIVE-TEST-OVERLAP-1] When no case configuration names a provider fixture, a run begins the production graph's preparation before the declaration check — loading its stored checked facts, or starting the `native-test-compile` session that checks it — so the body check and the declaration pass run side by side. A declaration failure, or a selection that never uses the graph, kills the session without forwarding its output, so the reported failure and exit status are exactly those of the ordered preparation.
- [NATIVE-TEST-FACTS-2] The `native-test-compile` session's facts describe only the transitive dependency closure of the functions a test run can select — every discovered test and every exported `bench_` function — with a schema of just their signatures, and the address-taken indirect-call target set is collected only once a described or selected body invokes a function value. Selective emission exports and describes only its selected roots and marks each selected name with one table lookup. The one-shot `native-test-graph` command keeps whole-program facts for `mylang invoke`.
- [NATIVE-TEST-PLAN-2] Planning reads each graph into an index once — symbol positions, resolved dependency edges and facts — so planning a selected test costs its own closure, and a module's contract identity is computed once for all of its tests; a malformed unit fails only the plans that reach it. The plan key is BLAKE3 in the `native-test-plan-v2` domain, so receipts written under the SHA-256 key miss once, and a published receipt leaves out the closure's symbol list, which its key already binds.
- [NATIVE-TEST-IDENTITY-1] The resolved source identity is computed through an import memo under `<cache>/imports/`, keyed by each module's content (BLAKE3) and whether it is the entry, and holding exactly the import names the project loader records for those bytes; a module the memo lacks is loaded alone to read its header, and a module or standard import the walk cannot resolve falls back to the full load. The memoized identity equals the full-load identity, and a damaged memo is ignored. End-of-run verification re-reads the captured tree and accepts it byte-for-byte without resolving imports again; only a changed tree is resolved again, so an unrelated neighbouring edit still never fails the run.
- [NATIVE-TEST-SUMMARY-1] The `native-test-compile` session reads its entry's check summaries the way the build frontend does (CHECK-SUMMARY-2): it checks every body, because its facts and its selective lowering read the checked tree, and a summary stands in only for an unchanged body's effect and mutation facts and pass diagnostics, so the passes walk only changed bodies; a request served by summaries reads no retained checked trees, and the session publishes summaries only when it had none or repeated in full.


## Guest performance benchmarks

- [NATIVE-BENCH-1] `mylang test --bench PATTERN` discovers exported `bench_` functions beside `test_` functions and reuses the same cases file for typed arguments, expectations, provider substitutions and named input sizes, each size becoming its own stable benchmark ID `name[size]`. Boolean results default to true and other result types require an explicit expectation, exactly as correctness tests do. Benchmark filtering is independent of test filtering; correctness tests run first and must pass before any measurement, and `--bench-only` skips them for local iteration. Malformed settings, unknown case keys and missing expectations reject instead of silently skipping a benchmark.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-2] Each benchmark invokes the complete native copied-value call, including argument and result codec costs, and measures it inside the native subprocess with a monotonic clock after warmup and calibration to a target batch duration. Compilation, process startup, library loading and report serialization are excluded from the declared timing. The harness reports raw batch durations and iteration counts, median nanoseconds per operation, interquartile spread and sample count, validates the returned value outside every timed region, and keeps the workload observable. Every operation is a fresh copied-value call, so no guest state carries between operations. A trap, an incorrect result, a timeout or an invalid output fails the run and is listed in the candidate's failures instead of producing a measurement.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-3] Three distinct versioned artifacts exist: the approved `perf/baseline.json` with measurements, inventory and provenance; `perf/policy.json` with default and per-benchmark limits, the required suite, runner profile and noise budget; and a fresh run report or candidate. Malformed data, unknown versions, duplicate benchmark IDs, invalid numbers and nonpositive timing baselines reject. A baseline is never replaced while it is being checked, candidate writes are atomic, and a candidate clearly states whether every required test and benchmark completed so an incomplete report cannot become an approved baseline. Each measurement carries its benchmark ID, workload version, fixture identity, measurement-boundary version, optimization mode, sampling configuration and runner profile, with source revision, compiler, toolchain, OS, CPU and environment recorded as provenance.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-4] Comparison is lower-is-better. A relative regression is flagged only when `(C - B) / B` exceeds `allowed_growth_percent / 100` and `C - B` exceeds `minimum_meaningful_delta_ns`; an independent `max_ns_per_op` ceiling applies even when relative growth is small; equality with a limit passes and zero percent tolerance is valid. Only runs whose workload version, fixture identity, boundary version, optimization mode, runner profile and sampling configuration match are compared, and the code-under-test implementation hash is deliberately not part of that key. Unbaselined, removed, skipped, incompatible and missing results produce explicit incomplete-coverage or incompatible verdicts rather than a green gate. A noisy or near-limit result earns at most one predefined confirmation run whose samples are evaluated together; unresolved noise is inconclusive and never silently passes.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-5] The approved baseline stays fixed until a reviewed promotion replaces it. Promotion requires a complete, passing candidate and records the approver, reason, revision, runner profile and old and new measurements in retained history, so successive accepted regressions stay visible and a successful build never advances the baseline by itself. A local comparison writes no baseline, a candidate cannot raise its own limits to pass, and the baseline and policy formats serve a later compiler benchmark adapter through distinct benchmark IDs and timing boundaries.
- [NATIVE-BENCH-6] A directory benchmark run discovers and measures each program's benchmarks with a fan-out width of one, so timing batches never compete with each other, and a failing correctness test in any child blocks that program's measurements. Because a directory run has no single aggregate benchmark report, it rejects `--bench-output`, `--bench-baseline` and `--bench-policy`; each child's most severe exit status carries its own performance gate.

## Guest memory ceilings

- [MEMORY-CEILING-1] Every process that runs guest code runs under a byte ceiling. The soft ceiling is the managed heap's own budget: an allocation that would exceed it after a collection traps with `R_MEMORY_BUDGET`, "memory budget exhausted", and exit status 1, rather than growing. The ceiling travels in `MYLANG_MEMORY_LIMIT` as bytes with an optional `K`, `M` or `G` suffix, so no spawner needs a new argument ABI and a program started by hand under an exported variable is bounded the same way; malformed text refuses the run instead of leaving it unbounded, and zero removes the ceiling. `--max-memory=BYTES` on `run`, `build --run` and `test` outranks the variable, which outranks the built-in default: 512 MiB for one test child, 4 GiB for `run` and `build --run`. A resolved test ceiling is part of a receipt key, so changing it re-executes rather than reuses.
- [BUDGET-HINT-1] A native budget trap reports the flag that raises it instead of a generic failure: `R_STEP_LIMIT` answers "instruction budget exhausted; raise it with --max-steps=N" and `R_FRAME_LIMIT` answers "call-depth budget exhausted; raise it with --max-frames=N", with or without a located guest trace, in text and in the message field of `--diagnostics=json`. Every other runtime code keeps the text it had, and `R_MEMORY_BUDGET` still answers "memory budget exhausted".

- [RUNTIME-UNBOUNDED-1] `mylang run`, `mylang build --run` and a binary built by `mylang build` count instructions and call depth without a default ceiling: a program is stopped only by `--max-steps=N`, `--max-frames=N`, the byte ceiling, or `--timeout=SECONDS`. An embedded library run with zeroed `MylangRunOptions` is unbounded the same way. `mylang test` keeps its per-test guard of 1,000,000 instructions and 1,024 frames.

- [RUNTIME-TIMEOUT-1] `--timeout=SECONDS` on `mylang run` and `mylang build --run`, or in a built binary's `MYLANG_OPTIONS` ([NATIVE-ARGV-1]), arms a monotonic wall-clock timer inside the guest; when it fires the process writes `R_TIMEOUT: wall-clock budget exhausted; raise it with --timeout=SECONDS` to standard error and exits 1. There is no default timeout, and a zero, empty or non-numeric value is a usage error.
- [MEMORY-CEILING-2] Behind the soft ceiling the runtime caps its own address space with `RLIMIT_AS` at the budget plus fixed headroom, before its first allocation, so a runtime defect cannot outgrow the budget. It never loosens a tighter ceiling already in force and installs none under a sanitizer, whose shadow mapping reserves more address space than any budget allows. The compiler bounds itself the same way at start-up, at 8 GiB unless `MYLANG_HOST_MEMORY_LIMIT` says otherwise, and the tools a native build spawns complete under the inherited ceiling. Every host that runs a guest in its own process — the byte runner, the board trial and the schematic renderer — installs that same ceiling before it runs anything, because there a runaway guest is a runaway host.
- [MEMORY-CEILING-3] A directory test run fans out over the cores the machine has, further limited by how many per-child ceilings its reported free memory holds, so a cold run on a loaded machine waits instead of oversubscribing. `--jobs N` outranks `MYLANG_JOBS`, which outranks that derived width; zero and non-numeric widths are rejected, the width never exceeds the programs there are to run, and it is never less than one.

See `docs/experiments/guest-tests.md` and `tests/native_test_cli.py`.

## Installed feature identity

- [FEATURE-REPORT-1] `mylang --features` reports `language-features-v1`, the compiler
identity, pinned Zig version and embedded feature/restriction manifest, without reading
its own executable. `compiler_sha256` is that identity: a build-time SHA-256 over the
compiler's source inputs, Zig version and optimize mode, embedded when the compiler is
built. The same identity appears in every receipt's `implementation`, so a receipt and a
feature report from one compiler always agree. The current reference is
`docs/current-language.md`; historical checkpoint prose does not supersede it. The
manifest records implementation coverage, not a test certificate.
- [CLI-HELP-1] `mylang --help`, `-h` and `help` print to stdout and exit 0: first a pointer to `mylang docs primer`, then the everyday commands (`check`, `run`, `build` and `build --release`, `test`, `fmt`, `docs`, `reference --query`), then a "Working in a large project" block naming the navigation commands that exist (`context --symbol`, `context --at`, `tests-for`, `list-interfaces`, `test --affected --against`), then the full command reference. Any other malformed invocation still prints the reference to stderr and exits 2.
- [CLI-DOCS-1] `mylang docs` answers from the user guides embedded in the executable, so a project outside the compiler repository can read them: with no argument it lists every topic with a one-line summary, `primer` first, and `mylang docs TOPIC` prints that guide's Markdown to stdout and exits 0 from any working directory; `--help` prints its usage, and an unknown topic or a second argument exits 2 naming the topics. The topics are the `docs/` pages one table in `src/user_docs.zig` names (`primer`, `syntax`, `statements`, `records`, `functions`, `errors`, `text`, `bytes`, `io`, `effects`, `modules`, `memory`, `arenas`, `stdlib`, `tests`, `build`, `navigation`, `context`); the build embeds each one, so an edited page ships with the next compiler and a moved one fails the build.
- [FEATURE-REPORT-2] The manifest's `implemented` section is a JSON object keyed by feature
slug. Every entry carries `kind`, either `language` or `tooling`, a one-line `summary`, and a
`doc` pointer of the form `<page>.md#<heading-anchor>` naming the documentation section that
demonstrates the feature. A slug that stops describing a real form moves to `removed` with a
date and a note rather than disappearing.
- [DOCCOV-1] `mylang doc-coverage [<repo-root>]` resolves every implemented feature's `doc`
pointer against that checkout. A pointer without an anchor, a page that does not exist, a page
reached through a `..` component, a page under `docs/legacy/` whose examples the runner never
executes, and an anchor matching no heading are all reported failures.
A heading's anchor is its text lowercased with punctuation dropped, spaces turned into hyphens,
and hyphens and underscores kept; its section runs to the next heading of the same or higher
level, and headings inside fenced blocks are not headings. A `language` section must contain a
fence tagged `mylang` or `my` that the documentation runner executes — one tagged
`# doc: skip` or `# doc: not-mylang` does not count. A `tooling` section must contain a fence
tagged `bash`, `sh`, `console` or `text`, or an executable program fence.
- [DOCCOV-2] Every `export` of every `src/stdlib/*.my` module must be named as a whole word in
that module's `## <module>` section of `docs/standard-library.md`, every such section must
carry an executed example, and every exported definition must have a `#` comment line directly
above it in the module source.
- [DOCCOV-3] The command prints per-page feature counts, the standard-module and export totals,
and one line per failure, then a failure count. It exits 0 only when nothing failed, 1 when
anything is undocumented, and 2 for an unusable command line. A missing or malformed
`features.json`, an unreadable page and an unlistable `src/stdlib` are reported as failures
rather than host errors. `zig build test-doc-coverage` runs it, and the default `test` step
depends on it.
- [DOCTEST-1] `mylang doctest [<repo-root>] [--filter GLOB] [--report PATH] [--verbose]` walks
`README.md`, `SPEC.md` and every `*.md` under `docs/` recursively, except `docs/legacy/`,
extracts fenced `mylang`/`my` blocks, and applies the leading `# doc:` directives (`expect`,
`expect-error`, `check`, `project`, `file`, `skip`, `not-mylang`, `allow-read`, `allow-write`).
Blocks sharing a `# doc: project <name>` are materialised as one multi-file project under a
private temporary directory; every unit is then checked, run, check-project-ed or run-project-ed
by the running compiler with `--diagnostics=json` and the same `--print-result` and
`--restrict` capability-grant options the documentation gate used before. `expect` compares
trimmed standard output, `expect-error` compares `diagnostic-v1` codes, and the command reports
per-document tallies, mis-tagged prose fences and failures. `--report PATH` writes a
`doc-examples-v1` JSON report, and the command exits 0 only when nothing failed, nothing was
mis-tagged and at least one executable block was found.
- [DOCTEST-2] Units are independent, so `mylang doctest` executes them on a bounded pool of
workers that pull from one shared cursor, each unit keeping its own private directory, child
process and status. The width is `--jobs N`, then `MYLANG_JOBS`, then the machine's cores capped
by how many per-child memory ceilings its free memory holds — the rule and the ceiling
`mylang test <dir>` fans out under; a zero or non-numeric width is a usage error. Statuses are
collected and reported only after every unit has finished, so the per-document tallies, the
failure lines and the summary are byte-identical to a serial run whatever order the units
complete in; `--verbose` progress lines interleave but each is written whole under one lock. A
host failure in one worker leaves the units the other workers hold running.
- [DOCTEST-3] A compiler child that dies in the host — the host exit status, no `diagnostic-v1`
  record, and either the CLI's host-failure marker or no program output at all — fails its
  example with a `host failure` detail even when the example expects no output, so a dropped
  example can no longer be counted as a run. A located source diagnostic, a guest that returns
  the host status after printing its result, and every other diagnosed failure keep their
  existing meaning.

- [DIAGNOSTIC-RECOVERY-1] Semantic checking recovers at ordinary and symbolic function boundaries after a source error, reports independent function faults in source order, and deduplicates repeated errors at the same source range and code. It stops within the failed function and skips whole-program verification when any body is incomplete; syntax, type-catalog, and namespace construction remain prerequisites. A failed check never publishes typed code or permits execution. Repeating a failed compilation check preserves diagnostics rather than appending duplicates.
- [DIAG-ORACLE-1] Type mismatches render the type the way source spells it, unknown fields and variants list the declared members, unknown functions suggest close candidate names, and arity errors report the expected and given argument counts.

## Proven parameter return regions

- [STANDARD-MODULES-7] Standard generic Result values retain the language's must-handle obligation; an ignored fallible result is a source error.

- [BYTE-IO-BULK-1] The byte application host optionally registers io::read_block(i64)->Block and io::write_block(Block)->i64 for a copied plain Block with bytes:[256]u8 followed by count:i64. Reads return up to 256 useful bytes with zero padding, count zero at EOF, and reject offsets outside [0,input length]. Writes reject counts outside [0,256], append up to remaining unpublished output capacity, and return the accepted count. Each call charges one work unit plus transferred bytes before mutation; a work failure never appends a partial block. The block's record and array shapes are validated against the emitted native host metadata before any callback is linked, and declaring only one of the pair rejects. Existing scalar declarations remain compatible. Output is published only after successful guest exit.
- [BYTE-IO-LIMITS-1] mylang-io accepts unique decimal --max-input, --max-output, --max-steps, --max-calls and --max-work values using = syntax after --project DIRECTORY. It also accepts unique --qbe=, --zig= and --cache-dir= paths for the native build, preferring an explicit path, then the environment, then the tools this build produced. Input/output limits may be zero; execution budgets must be positive. Unknown flags, duplicate limits, non-decimal values and overflow reject before invocation. The configured input limit, output publication capacity and guest call/work budgets are enforced without changing default limits.

- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-1] Packaged resource adapters accept only relative normal path components under an open directory and exact configured numeric TCP endpoints. Read-only acquisition rejects write/create/truncate modes before effects; network adapters do not resolve DNS or follow redirects.
- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-2] Directory adapters reject symlink path components, create exclusively, truncate only opened regular files, report actual partial I/O and close all provider handles when their stores end. The host keeps directory and endpoint grants alive until provider stores close.
- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-3] The packaged synchronous TCP adapter connects only after an exact endpoint grant match, transfers bounded byte chunks with reported progress, and closes the connection on completion or cleanup.

- [SCOPED-PROVIDERS-4] mylang-io provisions directory and numeric endpoint capabilities only with explicit --allow-dir=PATH and --allow-endpoint=IP:PORT arguments; directories default to read-only and --allow-write requires a directory grant. Unknown, duplicate directory, invalid endpoint and unscoped write grants reject before invocation.

- [LOOP-EXIT-1] break and continue are terminating statements targeting the nearest enclosing while loop. They reject outside loops, retain must-handle obligations, conservatively preserve ownership of outer locals, and release exited owned values and nested managed arenas before jumping. Generic bodies enforce the same contracts. Loops remain conservatively fallthrough-capable for return checking.

- [CONSTANT-1] Module-level const NAME: TYPE = EXPR declarations define immutable builtin integer or bool values. Initializers use literals, scalar casts/operators and visible constants with acyclic forward dependencies; allocation and external effects are excluded even in untaken operands; SOURCE-EVAL-1 adds bounded scalar source-function calls. Checking evaluates with width/overflow/division validation and bounded depth/work before emitting literal getters. Normal short-circuit evaluation applies after syntax/dependency validation. Exports and imported names use ordinary privacy checks; generic functions may read constants.
- [CONSTANT-2] Module-level str and bytes constants require a matching text or byte literal initializer, including empty literals and ordinary escapes; calls, concatenation, casts and dependencies are rejected even when unused. Transparent aliases resolve normally. Their getters retain ordinary checked literals and contribute managed_allocation on every read, including imported and generic reads; noalloc/effects() callers reject. Scalar constant evaluation is unchanged. Checking, run, build --run and installed executables agree, and literal edits invalidate cached semantics and emitted objects.

- [FOR-RANGE-1] for NAME in START..END iterates a half-open ascending range of matching integer types with a scoped binding the body may assign ([PY-FOR-REBIND-1]). Bounds evaluate once left-to-right; empty/reversed ranges execute no body. Continue performs the hidden checked increment before the next condition; break skips it. Generic ranges retain integer requirements and the same ownership cleanup rules as while.


- [TYPE-ALIAS-1] type NAME = TYPE declares a transparent non-parameterized alias for an existing scalar, aggregate, fixed array or concrete nominal application. Alias chains support forward references and reject cycles or declaration conflicts. They preserve target nominal identity, ownership and result obligations; they never unwrap domains. Exported aliases obey interface visibility checks and private aliases cannot be named across modules. Constants accept aliases of supported scalar types.



## Runtime requested-byte accounting

- [RUNTIME-BYTES-1] Embedded allocation budgets support child quotas clamped to the parent limit. Child allocations and resize/remap operations charge both live-byte limits; failed acquisitions do not charge storage; release restores live accounting and retains peak statistics. Accounting objects must outlive every allocation made through them.
- [INTEGER-EXTENDED-1] Named integer bitwise operations, shifts, wrapping and saturating arithmetic use declared widths; invalid shift counts trap identically across optimization modes.

## Explicit implementation obligations

- [TYPED-HOLES-1] `todo[T]()` declares an unresolved implementation obligation with a checked required type and a located `E_TYPED_HOLE` diagnostic plus related type span. Ordinary and uncalled generic function bodies report independent holes at safe recovery boundaries. Formatting retains them. No checked program, bytecode or executable is published until every hole is replaced. This is an explicit declared type, not inferred expected-type completion.

## Bounded host replay

## Bounded VM explanations


- [FLOAT-DOMAIN-1] Builtin float is IEEE binary64: separate arithmetic operations whose NaNs are canonical wherever they are observed ([FLOAT-NAN-OBSERVED-1]), numeric comparisons in which signed zeros compare equal and every NaN comparison is false, and checked conversion to and from signed integers. The fifteen raw `f64_*` source spellings are removed; ordinary `float` values use numeric operators and the prelude `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc` and `abs` operations. `src/stdlib/math.my` reaches the correctly rounded binary64 square root through the compiler-internal `__float_sqrt` spelling because the module declares its own `sqrt`, which shadows the prelude helper.
- [INTERNAL-NAME-1] The compiler-internal `__float_sqrt` spelling is accepted only in embedded standard-library sources; ordinary source receives `E_INTERNAL_NAME` and is directed to the public `sqrt` operation.
- [CLOCK-INTRINSIC-1] The compiler-internal `clock_now[R](capability)` and `random_u64[R](capability)` provider reads are accepted only in embedded standard-library sources; ordinary source receives `E_INTERNAL_NAME` and is directed to the `time` and `random` standard modules. The standard `time.now` and `random.from_entropy` wrappers lower to the same provider boundary, so no bytecode or native lowering changes.
- [FLOAT-INTRINSIC-FLOAT-1] The fifteen removed `f64_*` source spellings report `E_REMOVED_SPELLING`; ordinary `float` values use numeric operators, conversions and the prelude `sqrt`, `floor`, `ceil`, `trunc` and `abs` operations.

- [STRUCTURAL-COMPOSITE-1] Structural field and operation signatures substitute fixed arrays and nominal applications. Result obligations remain transferred by value. Invalid unused templates still reject.



- [CONST-LENGTH-1] Generic functions and nominal declarations accept explicit const N:u64 parameters, used as array lengths, forwarded constant arguments and u64 values in function bodies. Literal lengths are bounded to 1048576 and participate in specialization identity separately from type arguments. Symbolic checking preserves unknown lengths and rejects invalid unused templates, including type/value confusion and array literals whose length cannot be proved.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-1] Supported changed-source checks reuse independently owned checked function bodies with relocated spans, nodes, statements and locals; failed checks or snapshot allocation failures do not replace the last complete snapshot.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-2] Declaration/type/import identities invalidate body reuse, and complete region/effect/codec passes rerun after every body edit.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-16] Retained-body validation orders its checks by cost and reads the source once: the dependency digest and both stored lengths reject before any source byte is touched, and the stored region — which encloses the stored body as its trailing slice — is then compared against the fresh source in one pass that decides the body too, because equal region bytes and equal body and region lengths imply equal body bytes. No persisted digest covers the body bytes, so the byte comparison stays the authority rather than being replaced by a digest, and a same-length body edit still misses as a declaration-region change while an unchanged neighbour body is still reused; an entry whose stored shape does not place the body at the end of its region keeps its own body comparison, and the reason a rejected request reports is unchanged: a length change stays a region-length change, a dependency change stays a dependency change, and a changed body or region stays a declaration-region change.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-7] Ordinary checked-body eligibility is per function: an unrelated generic, alias, constant or callback declaration no longer disables reuse, and changing one ordinary body rechecks that body while unchanged dependents reuse. Entries are keyed by stable declaration identity with dependency digests over referenced declarations, types, layouts, constants, scope/import/visibility lookups including negative lookups, and effects/mutation/return-origin contracts. Old function and record ordinals remap to the fresh catalog; missing or ambiguous remapping is a miss, never a partial checked body. Global effect, mutation and codec passes still rerun after every body edit so a reused caller never keeps an obsolete callee verdict.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-8] Ordinary checked bodies persist across compiler processes as versioned `semantic-cache-v2` entries. The durable header/checksum and payload carry only stable declaration identities, dependency contexts, source-region/body bytes and owned remappable checked trees; every decoded reference/range/tag is validated and remapped through C1R or treated as a miss. Only successful checks publish atomically, incompatible compiler/effects/runtime-ABI/flags/mutation-contract versions are isolated, damaged/truncated/oversized entries fall back fresh, and `--no-cache` performs no semantic reads or writes while preserving acceptance and diagnostics.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-15] Durable checked-body publication batches a bounded group of entries and issues one shared filesystem synchronization when the batch closes, instead of a synchronous flush per body. Each entry is still written as a complete checksummed image and atomically renamed before that sync, updates stay proportional to the changed entries, a warm request that publishes nothing performs no sync, and `--no-cache` performs none either. A crash or an interrupted write can therefore leave only a torn image, which the checksum, version, contract, identity, context and bounds validation rejects as a miss; no partial or stale image is ever accepted.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-9] A checked-body snapshot larger than the in-memory retention budget keeps a bounded leading prefix of stable ordinary bodies and drops the remainder instead of disabling reuse for every body. Retained bodies reuse across edits; dropped bodies miss and check fresh on demand; a budget that fits no body preserves the previous complete snapshot only while it still fits this request's ordinary share, otherwise releases it so the combined bound holds.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-10] Relocating a retained ordinary body resolves exactly the declarations that body references. The remap is sized by the body's reference set, not the program catalog, and an old declaration ordinal the body did not carry is an `InvalidRelocation` miss.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-13] `check-session clear` releases every in-memory retention kind — ordinary checked bodies, symbolic templates and concrete specializations — together with their keys, indexes and arenas. Cumulative work counters stay monotonic and `functions.retained_bytes` reports the now-empty storage. Programs already returned from a cleared cache remain valid and deinitialization after clear is safe.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-14] Ordinary checked bodies, symbolic templates and concrete specializations share one `reuse.max_bytes` in-memory budget that accounts for owned entry rows, exact arena backing allocations including per-chunk bookkeeping, stable keys, and exact hash-index backing capacity including the stdlib minimum capacity, header and alignment. Newly checked publication and durable-hit promotion converge on the bound by releasing the globally oldest generic entry or truncating the ordinary snapshot to its fitting leading prefix; evicted entries miss and check fresh, oversized entries bypass retention without changing acceptance, a zero budget disables reuse, and `functions.retained_bytes` reports the total with a per-kind breakdown and eviction count.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-11] Checked symbolic generic templates persist their direct abstract results (effect/mutation masks, body-local diagnostics and ordered abstract call edges) across compiler processes under an explicit `symbolic-template-v1` payload kind and a stable key namespaced away from ordinary checked bodies. Replay requires byte-identical declaration region/body, a matching dependency context, name-resolution mode and abstract-checker version; scoped-bound resolution and structural validation still run on every request, and the whole-graph effect propagation always reruns from the combined fresh and replayed masks. A changed template body invalidates its own entries while an unrelated ordinary body edit preserves template reuse; malformed unused templates are always rechecked; `work-report-v1` reports real `symbolic_templates` checked/reused counts.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-12] Concrete specialization keys are canonical over the template identity, ordered positional type/length descriptors, transitive name-ordered provider contracts, template constraint dependencies, declared effect/mutation/return-origin contracts and the build context, with every boundary length-prefixed. Checked concrete specializations persist under an explicit `specialization-v1` payload kind in the shared `semantic-cache-v2` store and are replayed only when the canonical key, defining declaration region/body, dependency context and build contract match and every stored typed `FunctionRef` and record reference remaps to exactly one fresh identity. Missing, ambiguous, interned-catalog and synthesized-lambda references, and nested specialization references whose instance the fresh run has not yet materialized, are conservative misses; a self-recursive specialization remaps to its own fresh instance. Whole-graph effect and mutation propagation always reruns from the combined fresh and replayed trees. `--no-cache` performs no specialization reads or writes.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-3] Cached source spans and node/statement identities must belong to unchanged mapped intervals and their function's tables; invalid relocation falls back instead of trusting stale references.

- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-4] Reusing a caller body never reuses transitive effect verdicts; a callee's new effect rejects an unchanged effect-bounded caller.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-5] Failed cache publication releases only its pending snapshot; the previous successful function queries remain reusable after OutOfMemory.
- [SEMANTIC-CACHE-6] Project queries include module paths, import/export text, signatures and type catalogs, preserving fresh/cached diagnostics and execution through source relocation and changed module interfaces.
- [CALLBACK-1] Noncapturing function values have exact parameter/result signatures and effect ceilings, defaulting to unbounded when omitted, including parameter-dependent signatures in generic helpers. An implementation callable is assignable to a destination callable when its parameter count and types and its result type are exactly equal and its declared effect ceiling is a subset of the destination's; the reverse direction rejects. A call through a stored callback uses the destination ceiling. Creation stores an immediate function identity; invocation validates the target and preserves ordinary ownership/result obligations. Callback parameters and results are ordinary copied values; captured environments are rejected.

- [CALLBACK-EFFECTS-1] A callback type with no `effects(...)` ceiling is unbounded, and an unannotated free function is assignable to any callback slot; an explicit narrow ceiling still rejects a function value whose declared ceiling is wider, and a call through a stored callback uses the destination ceiling.
- [CALLBACK-SLOT-1] An unannotated function used as a value takes the effect ceiling of the callback slot it fills: a parameter known at the call, and equally a generic callee's callback parameter once its type arguments are inferred from the checked arguments (`sort.sort_by_key(xs, key)` with `key` unannotated). The function's inferred transitive body effects must fit that ceiling: a function value whose target has no clause of its own and whose body reaches an effect the slot forbids — `managed_allocation` included, so an allocating helper no longer fills an `effects()` slot a `noalloc` caller invokes — is `E_EFFECT_BOUND` at the value, naming the target, the slot type, the effect and its witness path, so no effect can reach a bounded caller through a callback. `mutation` is the mutation analysis's, an unbounded slot accepts any body, and a sort key that builds a `str` still fits because the key slot admits allocation (LAMBDA-KEY-INFER-1).

- [DEAD-RECORD-WRITE-1] Writing a field of a plain-`record` local that was copied from a collection read, a loop iteration variable, or a by-value parameter is rejected with `E_DEAD_RECORD_WRITE` when the copy is never read afterwards, because the write cannot reach the original storage; a field write to a definitely fresh local, or to a copy that is read again after the write, is accepted, and the diagnostic names `class` as the reference-semantics alternative.
- [CLASS-MANAGED-1] A `class` declaration is a managed reference record: construction allocates a GC-traced object, assignment, calls and field reads share that object, and a field write through any binding reaches the original; `record` remains a copied value type whose dead copy write is rejected by [DEAD-RECORD-WRITE-1].

- [SEMANTIC-CALLBACK-1] Function-name values may infer callback schemas while checking bodies, so semantic body reuse stays disabled for these programs until snapshots can safely remap their inferred types. Fresh and changed-source callback execution retain identical signatures and outcomes.

- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-1] Standard durations and Unix nanosecond instants use checked signed arithmetic that reports overflow, reversal and unrepresentable spans as Result failures instead of wrapping or trapping; seeded SplitMix64 has stable word sequences, a shared stream across copies of a generator, and inclusive integer sampling that covers the whole signed range without modulo bias.
- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-2] Standard clock and entropy calls require explicit capabilities and keep the unavailable, denied and failed provider categories apart in their reported messages; a later failed read leaves earlier results intact and host allocation failure aborts normally.
- [STANDARD-TIME-RANDOM-3] Standard provider wrappers preserve source effect ceilings, so a caller declaring a narrower ceiling than clock or random plus managed allocation is rejected while checking; one entropy-seeded generator performs exactly one provider read and replays identically from its state word.

- [TYPE-ALIAS-SCHEMA-1] Exported transparent aliases retain their checked canonical target in public schemas and native interfaces, including scalar, array, chained and concrete generic targets.



- [SCOPED-DIRECTORY-1] Read-authorized `m:path` metadata and `l:path` directory listing acquisitions publish bounded immutable byte snapshots under the existing directory grant. Empty paths denote that root only for these modes; intermediate/final directory traversal never follows symlinks, while final metadata reports a symlink itself.
- [SCOPED-DIRECTORY-2] Failed directory snapshot construction releases partial bytes and open directories before returning; allocation failures never publish a live snapshot handle.


## Standard library over managed types

- [STANDARD-MATH-1] Standard math classifies, rounds and roots binary64 values with IEEE behaviour preserved at zeros, infinities and not-a-number; sqrt reduces by exact powers of four and refines by Newton iteration, pow takes an integer exponent whose magnitude saturates by parity, and abs, gcd, lcm and isqrt report the values the signed range cannot hold as Result failures.
- [STANDARD-STRINGS-1] Standard strings add the classification, search, trimming, padding, case mapping and numeric conversion that builtin str lacks. `to_int` delegates to `int(text)` and `to_float` delegates to `float(text)`, so both wrappers use the pinned numeric grammar and parser; the float wrapper accepts surrounding whitespace, digit underscores, infinities and NaNs that its former guest parser rejected. `format_int` covers bases 2 to 36 including the most negative integer, and `format_float` rounds halves away from zero and rejects magnitudes it cannot render.
- [STANDARD-FORMAT-FLOAT-EXACT-1] `strings.format_float` rounds the exact binary value of its argument: when the scaled product `|value| * 10**decimals` rounds onto an exact half (for example `2.675 * 100.0 == 267.5`), the exact product error decides, so `format_float(2.675, 2)` is `"2.67"` as in C and Python. Only a value whose scaled product is exactly a half rounds away from zero (`format_float(0.125, 2)` is `"0.13"`), and a result that rounds to zero carries no sign (`format_float(-0.001, 2)` is `"0.00"`).
- [STANDARD-STRINGS-2] `strings.repeat(text, times)` doubles its copies instead of appending one copy at a time: the answer so far is appended to itself while its copy count stays within `times`, and one prefix of it tops up the rest, so building an answer of `n` octets copies at most about `3n` octets and takes a logarithmic number of concatenations, where the old loop copied the growing answer once per copy and was quadratic in `times`. An empty `text` or a zero count answers the empty text at once; a negative count is still `Error.Invalid`. Every answer is the same text.
- [STANDARD-SORT-1] Standard ordering is a stable merge over a copy that leaves its input unchanged, for integers, binary64 values, Unicode code point order on text, and any element type through an integer key callback. The concrete integer, float and text wrappers delegate to the same one-work-buffer engine as `sorted`, and the keyed form to the keyed sort (STANDARD-SORT-KEY-1). Text comparison reads stored Unicode scalars directly without allocating a byte encoding. Binary search, extrema and duplicate removal report absence through Option and keep first-occurrence order.
- [STANDARD-PATH-1] Standard path operations are pure text algebra with no filesystem authority: joining honours an absolute right side, basename and dirname agree with the trailing-separator convention, splitext ignores a leading dot, and normalize removes duplicate separators, single dots and resolvable parents while keeping unresolvable ones.
- [STANDARD-CODECS-1] Standard base64 emits canonical padding for both RFC 4648 alphabets and decodes only canonical input, rejecting a length that is not a multiple of four, a symbol outside the alphabet, padding before the final group and a final group with nonzero unused bits. Hexadecimal transcoding accepts either letter case and rejects odd lengths.
- [STANDARD-JSON-1] Standard JSON parses strict RFC 8259 text into a recursive union, rejecting comments, trailing commas, leading zeros, bare fractions and exponents, unescaped control characters, lone surrogates, duplicate object keys, trailing text and nesting past a fixed limit, and naming the byte offset in every message. Objects keep parsed key order, a number without fraction or exponent that fits the signed range stays exact, and rendering round-trips a parsed document without added whitespace.
- [STDLIB-ERROR-UNION-1] A standard module whose operations can fail exports an error union and returns `Result[T, Module.Error]` from every exported fallible operation instead of `Result[T, str]`; each case carries a `str` payload reachable through an exported `message(error)` helper, so callers match on category instead of the human text and adding a case is a breaking interface change. `json` and `strings` are converted; `math`, `time`, `random`, `base64` and `hex` still return `Result[T, str]`.
- [STANDARD-POOL-1] A standard pool hands out copyable generational handles into a reusable slot store. Removing a slot advances its generation so every earlier handle stops matching; a stale or out-of-range handle reports absence and no handle traps, released slots are reused before the store grows, and copies of a pool value refer to one shared store. A handle names a slot and a generation rather than a pool, so it must be used with the pool that issued it.
- [STANDARD-SORT-KEY-1] `sort.sort_by_key(values, key)` is a thin forwarder to `sorted(values, key=key)` (LIST-SORT-KEY-1), so it returns a new stable list and leaves its input unchanged. Each key is computed exactly once, in input order — earlier versions computed them in the order a recursive merge first asked for each one, so a key function that traps on several elements now raises the trap of the first such element in input order, as Python does. The extra memory is the result, one key word per element and the native sort's scratch, for lists of any length.

## Application library parity

- [STANDARD-INTEGER-EXTREMA-1] Standard math integer min, max and clamp compare exact values at every supported integer width without arithmetic overflow, including both ends of each signed and unsigned range.

## Ordinary JSON schema helpers




## Bytecode execution

- [VM-AUDIT-1] Scalar-loop execution preserves results, exact instruction budgets and located traps across runtime representation optimizations.

- [BYTECODE-INLINE-1] Lowering inlines a direct call to a non-entry leaf function whose every instruction is on a no-fault, no-allocation allow-list, whose locals need no owned-resource cleanup, and whose owner scope (when any) matches the caller's. Splicing remaps the callee's locals and branch targets to fresh caller slots and the continuation pc, preserving results, trap traces and GC roots; a call to a fallible or allocating callee is left intact.

- [BYTECODE-INLINE-2] Lowering also inlines a direct call to a non-entry leaf function that can trap but cannot allocate: the allow-list admits bounds-checked scalar `get_index`, integer `binary_integer` and the checked add/sub/mul/div/rem, `cast_integer`, `neg_*`, the float conversions and `sqrt`-style operations of `unary_float` (the pure `myl_qbe_float_unary`), `array_length`, `get_field`, `update_record`, `switch_variant` and predicates, while `str` indexing and every call, managed construction, buffer, arena, print and file/resource/provider operation stay out. Every spliced instruction records its callee, callee-local pc and call-site pc; the emitter groups those into an inline-frame table linked into the function metadata, and the runtime walks it to emit the callee frames followed by the enclosing frame at the call site, innermost first, under the same sixteen-frame cap and `omitted_frames` accounting. Trap text, `MYLANG-TRAP` JSON and source locations stay byte-identical to the pre-inlining compiler, including nested inlines; only the step budget and non-entry `--max-frames` attribution still differ, because a splice consumes no logical frame yet.
- [BYTECODE-INLINE-3] A callee larger than the flat inline budget is spliced only where the call site runs inside a loop of the caller: the inliner marks every instruction in a loop body — the contiguous pc range that ends at a backward branch, which the structured lowering guarantees — and a larger callee is inlined there while the same callee's single-shot call sites keep their call, so a hot path loses its call while a cold path keeps its code size. A spliced larger callee gets the same provenance, inline-frame replay and trap text as a smaller one.

- [NATIVE-TIMING-1] `run --timings` reports native execution nanoseconds on stderr, covering the guest process and the native build it needed but excluding source compilation. Timing preserves guest results, quotas and runtime diagnostics; duplicate options and unsupported commands reject it.

- [PHASE-TIMING-1] One-shot compiler commands and the raw byte I/O host accept opt-in `--timings=json`, emitting versioned phase-timings-v1 JSON Lines on stderr. Records use nonnegative duration_ns, explicit inclusive scope, available work counts and the command exit_code; total includes cleanup and excludes timing serialization. Attempted failing phases remain observable, stdout and default behavior are preserved, and legacy `--timings` remains compatible.

- [PHASE-TIMING-2] Opt-in project timing may additionally separate source/header load, syntax replay and parse, declarations/interfaces, source merge, dependency-key preparation, ordinary-body replay, symbolic templates, concrete-specialization replay, fresh body checking, effect/origin passes, warning derivation and semantic retention/durable-store I/O. These records are bounded, disabled by default, never affect cache keys, acceptance, diagnostics or work-report counts, and use `inclusive=false` for disjoint phases while only `total` is inclusive.
